name: using-superpowers
description: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions

<SUBAGENT-STOP>
If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, ignore this skill.
</SUBAGENT-STOP>

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.

**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.

Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.

- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support

Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):

```toml
[features]
multi_agent = true
```

This enables `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent`, and `close_agent` for skills like `dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development`. When using subagent-driven-development, close reviewer subagents when their review returns. Keep each implementer subagent open until its task's review passes — the fix loop resumes the implementer — then close it. If your harness cannot send another message to a spawned agent, dispatch each fix round as a fresh implementer carrying the brief, the report file, and the findings.

## Environment Detection

Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:

```bash
GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
```

- `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
- `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)

See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.

## Codex App Finishing

When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
the user to use the App's native controls:

- **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
- **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout

The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.

---
name: multi-model-verify
description: Use when planning a reference port, an API-sensitive module, or any change risky enough to need cross-model verification before implementation, or when an implemented diff must be checked against its plan and reference before merge. Fires automatically alongside superpowers requesting-code-review via the review-companion hook.

# Multi-Model Verify

## Overview

Two equal-weight advisors — Fable 5 (this session) and GPT-5.6 Sol (via the
codex CLI) — verify and refute each other's claims before the cheap
implementer touches code. Sol's documented fabrication risk (METR) is
mitigated by the debate structure — evidence grounding plus mutual
refutation — not by down-weighting either side.

**REQUIRED READING before the first round:** references/debate-protocol.md.

Companion to superpowers, not a replacement: it fills the cross-model review
gap superpowers rules out of scope. `/codex:adversarial-review` remains the
human-invoked pre-merge gate; if the codex plugin's stop-review-gate is
toggled on, its stop-time review overlaps mode `diff` — expected, not a bug.

## When to use

- **Mode `plan`** — after superpowers brainstorming proposes approaches for a
  port or API-sensitive module, before the implementation plan is written.
- **Mode `diff`** — after implementation, alongside superpowers
  requesting-code-review (the review-companion hook injects this
  automatically with the matching base/head SHAs).
- NOT for plain bugfixes, docs or release chores, GUI tweaks, or lint sweeps.

## Preflight (both modes)

1. `codex --version` must succeed — on failure follow references/fallbacks.md
   (degraded mode, visibly flagged; never silently skip cross-vendor review).
2. For port work: the reference source must exist under `References/`
   (quote paths — some contain spaces, e.g. `References/M+ Timer`). Missing
   → **HARD STOP before any debate round runs**: ask the user for the path
   and go no further — do not run a codex exchange about the absence, do
   not debate from memory, do not start a degraded mode. If the folder
   holds version subdirectories (e.g. a `v1.4/` next to a `v1.1_old/`), use
   the newest unless the user names one, and cite the versioned path:
   `References/<name>/<version>/<file>:<line>`.
   For non-port work there is no reference folder — claims ground in the
   project's own source, specs, or upstream docs instead, same strike rule.

## Mode plan

1. Draft the Fable position: chosen approach, port-fidelity claims, and the
   API/behavior risk register. Every reference claim cites a
   `References/<name>/<file>:<line>` actually read this session — anchor
   EVERY file with its full path the first time you cite it, manifests and
   secondary files included (shorthand only once that file is anchored);
   every API claim cites authoritative local docs (WoW projects:
   `.wow-api-reference/` or a dated in-game probe result) — never memory.
   The position has no separate artifact — it IS the claims section of the
   step-2 brief.
2. Compose Sol's debate brief per references/model-prompting-notes.md, write
   it to a scratchpad file, then run round 1:

   ```powershell
   Get-Content -Raw <brief-file> | codex exec --sandbox read-only -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message <reply-file> -
   ```

   `<canonical-model-id>` and `<canonical-effort>` are the two declarations
   in references/model-prompting-notes.md — read the literal values from
   there, never from memory (the reviewer swaps by editing that one file,
   and a remembered id silently defeats the swap).

   Capture the `session id:` line printed in the run header (top of output);
   read Sol's reply from `<reply-file>` — the stdout transcript logs every
   file Sol reads and can run tens of KB, with the reply buried at the
   bottom.
3. Later rounds keep Sol's state by resuming that session — flags MUST
   precede the resume subcommand (flags after it are a usage error):

   ```powershell
   codex exec --sandbox read-only -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message <reply-file> resume <SESSION_ID> "<rebuttal-brief>"
   ```

4. Iterate per debate-protocol.md until convergence or the round cap, then
   escalate any unresolved points to the user with both positions stated.
5. Freeze the converged plan per references/frozen-plan-format.md under the
   project's superpowers plans dir (KitnEssentials:
   `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`; other projects: the superpowers default
   `docs/superpowers/plans/`).

## Mode diff

Same transport and protocol. First read the frozen plan's debate record and
its **Verification status** field. Then the brief carries the frozen plan
path, the base/head SHAs superpowers code review used, and the
`git diff base..head` output. Both sides check **spec fidelity** (drift from
the frozen plan — the implementer makes zero judgment calls, so any drift is
a finding) and, for port work, **port fidelity** (drift from the reference
source), ending PASS / FIX / ESCALATE.

**Degraded-plan poisoning rule:** a plan whose Verification status is
DEGRADED cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS. Mode diff must first reopen
and cross-verify the plan's claims and approved deviations (the
retrospective pass the plan never got), and only then verify the
implementation against it — confirming a diff matches an unsound plan
verifies nothing. If cross-vendor verification is STILL unavailable, the
only terminal state is `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`: merging
then requires the user's explicitly recorded risk acceptance, never a PASS.

## Finish line

Before it: the session's final-adjudication step (debate-protocol.md) —
verify the last round's findings against the repo and issue the terminal
verdict; a reviewer's PASS/FIX is never terminal by itself.

End with one status line: participating models, rounds used, converged vs
escalated points, and the verification status — `FULL`, or
`DEGRADED (<class>, authorized by user at round N)` per fallbacks.md. The
session emits this line itself; never delegate it to a subagent.

## Common mistakes

- Accepting Sol's claims about reference code without the cited lines —
  strike the claim per protocol; do not argue against it.
- Re-sending the full debate context each round instead of resuming the
  codex session.
- Running mode `diff` against different SHAs than the code review used.
- Treating convergence as failure — a sound plan converging in one round is
  the system working, not a skipped debate.

# Debate protocol

Both advisors are equal weight. Neither model's claim outranks the other's;
only evidence does.

## Evidence grounding (the strike rule)

- **Every externally checkable claim must carry a citation to a source the
  other side can read**: a file:line in the repo or reference material the
  citing side actually read (Fable: read this session; Sol: read inside its
  sandboxed run — its quoted excerpt counts as reading), authoritative local
  docs, or a fetched URL.
- Source-specific examples: reference-port claims cite
  `References/<name>/<file>:<line>`; WoW 12.0 API claims cite
  `.wow-api-reference/` or a dated in-game probe result; library or platform
  claims cite the vendored source or official docs.
- **Anchor every file the first time you cite it** — full repo-relative path
  with line, `References/DemoWidget/DemoWidget.toc:1`, not `DemoWidget.toc:1`.
  This holds for EVERY file, including one-line manifests and secondary
  files, not just the main source. Once a file is anchored, shorthand
  (`Core.lua:19`) is fine for later citations of that same file. A bare
  filename does not locate anything for the other side: the project has its
  own `DemoAddon.toc` and the reference has `DemoWidget.toc`, and an
  unanchored `.toc:1` could be either.
- A claim with no citation is **struck, not debated**. Note it as struck and
  move on — arguing against an ungrounded claim launders it into the record.
- Reports are claims requiring evidence. "The reference handles this" without
  the file:line is an assertion, not a finding.

## Round structure

Each exchange (one Fable position or rebuttal + one Sol reply) is a round.

1. From round 2 on, state position changes since last round: which of the
   other side's points are **accepted** (absorbed into the plan), which are
   **refuted** (with the evidence that refutes them), which are **struck**
   (no citation).
2. Raise new risks only with evidence attached.
3. End with a verdict on the current plan/diff:
   - **PASS** — no remaining substantive objections.
   - **FIX** — named change required, with the specific fix and its evidence.
   - **ESCALATE** — a disagreement evidence cannot settle (taste, missing
     in-game data, ambiguous reference intent).

## Convergence and the round cap

- Converged: both sides verdict PASS in the same round.
- **Converged with amendments**: the final round's only outstanding verdicts
  are FIXes whose fixes the other side accepts on the record. Record each
  accepted fix in the debate record and treat the plan as converged — an
  accepted FIX is agreement, not a dispute for the user to settle. (This
  matters whenever a FIX lands in the last round before the cap.)
- Round cap: **4 exchanges** by default (caller may raise or lower it).
  Hitting the cap is not failure — it is the signal to stop spending tokens
  on an argument evidence hasn't settled.
- At the cap or on ESCALATE, for points that remain genuinely contested:
  stop, present BOTH positions to the user with their evidence, and let the
  user decide. Never silently pick a side.
- Questions only runtime testing can answer always escalate — neither model
  can run the live application (in WoW projects, /reload + BugSack is always
  the user's step).

## Final adjudication (the session's last step)

The chain never ends on the external reviewer's verdict. After Sol's final
round — including a re-review of fixes — the SESSION performs one closing
step before anything merges or freezes:

1. Verify each finding from that final round against the live repo (read
   the cited lines; run the gates if implicated) — a final-round finding is
   itself an unverified claim until checked.
2. Accept with evidence, refute with evidence, or ESCALATE to the user.
   Nothing is adopted or dismissed on authority.
3. Emit the terminal verdict and the finish line. A reviewer PASS/FIX is
   input to this step, never the decision itself.

Equal weight governs claims during the debate; adjudication is procedural,
not a rank: the session is the accountable party with live-repo access, so
the session always has the final say — and a genuine deadlock still goes to
the user, never to whoever spoke last (user directive, 2026-07-12).

## Anti-sycophancy, anti-theater

Debates fail two ways: rubber-stamping and manufactured conflict. Both are
defects.

- **A sound plan gets one line.** "Plan is sound; the one thing to watch is
  X." Converging in round 1 is the system working.
- **Do not manufacture objections** to justify the round. If you would not
  push back in a real review, do not push back here.
- Do not restate the other side's point in different words as a "new" risk.
- Do not concede a point you can refute just to converge faster —
  unresolved disagreement has an honest exit (ESCALATE).

## Why the structure carries the trust

GPT-5.6 Sol has a documented (METR, OpenAI system card) tendency to fabricate
results under evaluation pressure. The mitigation is this protocol — every
claim must survive citation and refutation by an independent model — not
down-weighting Sol's verdicts. The same rules bind Fable equally: fabricated
detail is a known failure mode of every frontier model, and this project's
history (see memory: no-fabrication feedback) treats invented API behavior as
the cardinal sin.

===== C:\Users\Brandon\.codex\plugins\cache\crosscheck\crosscheck\0.5.0\skills\multi-model-verify\references\frozen-plan-format.md =====
# Frozen plan format

The debate's output is a superpowers-compatible implementation plan. The
implementer (Sonnet 5 or the session model, via superpowers
subagent-driven-development or executing-plans) follows it with **zero
judgment calls** — anything the plan leaves open is a plan defect, found in
mode `diff` as drift.

## Base format

Follow the superpowers writing-plans template exactly (header with Goal /
Architecture / Tech Stack / Global Constraints; bite-sized checkbox tasks
with exact Files / Interfaces / complete code / exact commands / expected
output; no placeholders). Save location: the superpowers default
`docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`, unless the project
overrides it (example: KitnEssentials uses `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`).

Port-specific Global Constraints to copy in verbatim when the work is a
port (KitnDev-family example — adapt the specifics per project):

- Project naming/style conventions the port must follow (KE example:
  KE namespace, flat DB keys, `KE:ApplyFontToText` for SOFTOUTLINE-capable
  text, string literals only — see the module-port skill).
- Reference source and version the port tracks:
  `References/<name>/` at the fidelity the debate agreed on (faithful core
  logic; project-specific deviations listed one per line WITH the debate
  round that approved each).
- Platform/API constraints the debate verified, each with its citation
  (KE example: WoW 12.0 secret-value rules).

## Debate record appendix (REQUIRED)

Every frozen plan ends with:

```markdown
---

## Debate record

**Participants:** Fable 5 (session) / GPT-5.6 Sol (codex exec, session <id>)
**Rounds used:** N of CAP
**Outcome:** converged | converged with amendments | escalated
**Verification status:** FULL | DEGRADED
**Degradation:** none | codex-missing | model-rejected | auth-expired | quota-exhausted | continuity-lost | <class>
**Authorized by:** n/a | user at round N | not-authorized

### Resolved points
| # | Claim | Raised by | Outcome | Evidence |
|---|-------|-----------|---------|----------|
| 1 | ...   | Sol       | accepted into Task 3 | References/<addon>/<file>:<line> |
| 2 | ...   | Fable     | refuted  | .wow-api-reference/<file> |
| 3 | ...   | Sol       | struck (no citation) | — |

### Escalated points (user-decided)
| # | Question | Fable position | Sol position | Owner's call |
|---|----------|----------------|--------------|--------------|

### Degraded-mode note
(required whenever Verification status is DEGRADED — name what was skipped,
why, and the recorded user authorization. The Participants line must name
the actual participants, never the default Sol line.)
```

The three status fields are structured on purpose: mode `diff` parses
`Verification status` to enforce the degraded-plan poisoning rule (see
SKILL.md) — prose notes cannot be enforced.

The appendix is the audit trail: mode `diff` re-reads it to know which
deviations were approved and which claims were struck. A frozen plan without
the appendix was not verified — treat it as an unfrozen draft.

## Freezing

A plan is frozen when: both sides verdict PASS — or the final round's FIXes
are accepted on the record (converged with amendments, per
debate-protocol.md) — with any escalations carrying the user's recorded
call, the writing-plans self-review checklist has run, and the file is
saved. After freezing, changes require reopening the debate
(a new round appended to the record) — the implementer never edits the plan.

===== C:\Users\Brandon\.codex\plugins\cache\crosscheck\crosscheck\0.5.0\skills\multi-model-verify\references\fallbacks.md =====
# Fallbacks and degraded modes

**Governing rule: no transition that reduces vendor diversity, evidence
quality, or conversation continuity happens without explicit user consent.**
The skill never enters degraded mode automatically. Degradation that is not
consented to is a stop, not a mode.

## The consent gate

When a failure survives bounded recovery (below), STOP and present a banner
to the user — before any degraded work happens:

```text
== CROSS-VENDOR VERIFICATION UNAVAILABLE ==
What failed:      <exact command + error>
What degraded mode would verify:     <e.g. claims via a fresh Fable skeptic>
What it would NOT verify:            <cross-vendor independence>
Options: [fix codex] [run degraded] [abort]
```

- The user's choice is recorded in the debate record (`Authorized by:` field
  — see frozen-plan-format.md).
- **Unattended runs fail closed**: if no user can answer, terminate as
  `BLOCKED/DEGRADED-NOT-AUTHORIZED` with a non-success status. Never wait
  indefinitely, never infer consent.

## Bounded recovery (automatic, consent-free)

One retry with the SAME model, sandbox, effort, and session parameters is
the DEFAULT recovery for any failure that has no named immediate-gate rule
below (codex-missing, model-rejected, auth-expired, and quota-exhausted go
straight to the gate — retrying those changes nothing). The retry reduces
nothing, so it needs no consent. A failed retry goes to the consent gate.
This covers timeouts and transient transport errors.

**Catch-all: any codex failure not named in this file — nonzero exit, empty
reply file, malformed output, network loss, resume failure —
gets the same treatment: one same-parameters retry, then the consent gate.**
No failure class is ever an implicit license to degrade.

## Failure classes

### codex CLI missing or broken
`codex --version` fails → consent gate immediately (there is nothing to
retry). If the user chooses degraded mode: replace each Sol round with a
FRESH-context skeptic subagent (Fable, read-only tools) — fresh context is
mandatory; same-context self-critique rubber-stamps (see
model-prompting-notes.md). Brief the skeptic with the same XML-style brief
Sol would have received, including the strike rule and the
anti-manufactured-objection rule.

### Sol model rejected (400 "not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account")
The account tier lost Sol access (free/Go tiers get Terra only; Plus and
above get Sol). Do NOT silently substitute another model — Sol is a user
directive. Consent gate: fix subscription / run degraded / abort.
(`gpt-5.6-terra` responding while `gpt-5.6-sol` 400s confirms tier-gating,
not a CLI problem — probed 2026-07-12.)

### Usage limit reached (session or weekly quota) — class `quota-exhausted`
The ChatGPT account's usage quota is exhausted; codex's error names which
limit was hit (the ~5-hour session window vs the weekly cap) and when it
resets. **Skip the retry** — nothing about a quota window is transient, and
a retry only burns another attempt against it. Go straight to the consent
gate with codex's reset time quoted verbatim in the `What failed:` line;
here the "fix codex" option means *wait for the reset* or *upgrade the
tier* — record which. If a debate is mid-flight, also note the codex
session id in the debate record so the debate resumes after the reset
instead of restarting from round 1.

### codex auth expired
`codex login status` fails → the fix needs the user anyway (`codex login`
is an interactive browser sign-in): consent gate.

### Session id lost or resume fails
Losing the resumed session degrades conversation continuity — an advertised
protocol property — so it is NOT automatic: one retry, then the consent
gate with the specific option "continue with fresh per-round Sol calls
(full brief re-sent each round; costs tokens, loses Sol's debate memory)".

### Stale API evidence
If the project's API-reference drift check reports the build changed under
a claim, that claim is **struck until re-verified** — the strike rule does
not weaken to a flag. Re-verify against the updated reference (or an
in-game/runtime probe) before the claim re-enters the debate.

## Degraded-mode output requirements (after consent)

- The SUPERVISING SESSION emits the banner — never delegate the banner to
  the skeptic subagent's output, or a formatting miss becomes a quiet path.
- Every round after the transition starts with
  `== DEGRADED (single-vendor) ==`.
- The finish line and the frozen plan carry the structured fields
  (`Verification status: DEGRADED`, `Degradation: <class>`,
  `Authorized by: user at round N`) per frozen-plan-format.md.
- The debate record's Participants line must name the actual participants
  (e.g. `Fable 5 (session) / Fable 5 skeptic subagent (DEGRADED -
  codex-missing)`), never the default Sol template line.
- A DEGRADED-frozen plan poisons downstream PASSes — see SKILL.md mode
  `diff` for the enforcement rule.

## Reference failures (not degraded modes — hard stops)

### Reference source missing for a port
Ask the user for the path. NEVER proceed with an ungrounded debate — a
debate about remembered reference behavior is two models fabricating at
each other. This is a hard stop, not a degraded mode.

## Known limits (documented, not silent)

- The review-companion hook is ADVISORY: it injects the diff-mode reminder;
  it cannot block a merge. The enforcement backstop is the finish rule and
  the user. It is also registered for failed review dispatches
  (PostToolUseFailure), so a crashed code review still surfaces the gate.
- Hook fingerprint rot is detected by the eval suite (pinned template
  fixture in CI + installed-template canary locally), not at runtime.

## Alternative transport (documented, rejected for v1)

CLIProxyAPI (the Theo/Tibo "claudex" setup) runs a SEPARATE Claude Code
process pointed at a local proxy that translates to the Codex backend
(same ChatGPT OAuth), with env vars like `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`
selecting Sol — it does not touch the existing Fable session's traffic.
Revisit only if codex exec proves limiting (e.g. a cross-vendor implementer
lane needs Claude Code's own tool harness). It does NOT bypass the
subscription tier gate — same auth, same 400 on free accounts. Costs: a
local proxy service to run and keep patched, a second configured process,
and untested cross-vendor hook and permission semantics.

===== CLAUDE.md =====
# parallax — Claude Code plugin (NOT a WoW addon)

This repo is developer tooling: a Claude Code plugin providing cross-model
verification (session ⇄ cross-vendor reviewer debates; the reviewer lane is
declared in the skill's model-prompting-notes.md) plus its eval harness. It lives
under KitnDev for convenience, but the WoW addon family conventions in
`../AGENTS.md` (dev loop, /reload, luacheck/busted, 12.0 API rules,
References/) do NOT apply here — only the git basics do (feature branches
for real work, lowercase imperative commits, no AI attribution).

## Verification
- `python evals/tools/skill_lint.py skills/multi-model-verify --strict`
- `python evals/tools/skill_scanner.py skills`
- `python evals/tools/run_trigger_evals.py`
- `python -m pytest evals -q`
CI runs all four on every push (.github/workflows/skill-evals.yml).

Two suites are local-only and opt-in, so run them by hand when you touch
what they cover:
- skill/prompt changes -> `python evals/tools/run_behavioral_evals.py`
  (real headless runs, graded by the cross-vendor reviewer; `--head` tests
  the checkout instead of the installed cache; for small contract edits
  `--changed` runs only cases whose declared surface intersects the diff
  vs main, printing every skip by name).
- `tools/check-drift.ps1` changes -> run
  `evals/tools/drift_statemachine_tests.ps1` (or, to go through pytest:
  `$env:PARALLAX_STATEMACHINE = "1"; python -m pytest evals -q` — it is
  PowerShell, so no `VAR=1 cmd` prefix). Drives the real script through its
  whole state machine offline against stub CLIs. Slow: four scenarios
  re-run the full pytest suite inside a disposable worktree.

## Dev loop
The plugin is installed user-scope from a LOCAL marketplace pointing at
this working copy, but installs are VERSIONED CACHE COPIES — checkout
edits are NOT live until you: bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, run
`claude plugin update parallax@parallax` (qualified name required),
and restart the session when hooks/ or skills/ changed. A restart alone
only reloads the cached version. GitHub remote (Bmwascher/parallax,
private) serves stable installs on other machines.

## Skill editing rules
The multi-model-verify skill's transport commands (codex exec flags, resume
syntax) are LIVE-VERIFIED contracts locked by evals/multi-model-verify/
test_multi_model_verify.py — change the tests first (they encode review
findings), then the skill.

   1: # Flash implementer lane (Antigravity CLI) — design

   7:

   9: `model: sonnet`). The user's roadmap (backlog item 4, order fixed 2026-07-25)
  10: replaces that build-task lane with Gemini Flash — "it does nothing but EXACTLY
  11: what you tell it to," a fit for a zero-judgment executor. Agent frontmatter
  12: only accepts Claude models, so Flash arrives by delegation: a cheap Claude
  13: tier holds the agent seat and drives a vendor CLI headlessly — the
  14: fable-advisor v3 `grok-implementer` pattern already documented in
  15: `implementer.md`'s Lane note.

  17: ## Transport decision: Antigravity CLI, not gemini-cli (user, 2026-07-25)
  18: 
  19: The obvious transport died during probing:
  20: 
  21: - gemini-cli 0.52.0 OAuth for individuals is server-side dead ("This client
  22:   is no longer supported for Gemini Code Assist for individuals... migrate to
  23:   the Antigravity suite"); six open issues confirm (primary #28229, P1).
  24:   Google's transition post: free/consumer serving ended 2026-06-18; only
  25:   paid/enterprise API keys remain supported.
  26: - Worse: with a still-working consumer API key, gemini-cli **silently served
  27:   gemini-3.5-flash when pinned to gemini-3.6-flash** (stats block evidence),
  28:   while a garbage model ID errored loudly — a silent model substitution, the
  29:   exact route-integrity failure the codex lane's effective-route check exists
  30:   to reject.
  31: - Antigravity CLI (`agy`) is the official successor (Google Developers Blog,
  32:   "Transitioning Gemini CLI to Antigravity CLI"), installed and probed
  33:   end-to-end the same night. gemini-cli plays no part in this design.
  34: 
  35: ## Probe record (agy 1.1.7, Windows, 2026-07-25)
  36: 
  37: All probes ran headless in a scratch directory; log evidence retained in the
  38: session scratchpad (`agy-probe/probe*.log`).
  39: 
  40: - Auth: Google Sign-In (system keyring), one-time interactive. `agy models`
  41:   requires sign-in and lists `gemini-3.6-flash-{high,medium,low}` (plus 3.5
  42:   variants, `gemini-3.1-pro-*`, `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `claude-opus-4-6-thinking`,
  43:   `gpt-oss-120b-medium` — the CLI is multi-vendor).
  44: - Pinned headless run: `agy -p "..." --model gemini-3.6-flash-low --log-file F`
  45:   → correct reply, exit 0; log carries route-request evidence:
  46:   `Print mode: starting (... model="gemini-3.6-flash-low" ...)` and
  47:   `Propagating selected model override to backend: label="Gemini 3.6 Flash (Low)"`.
  48:   Both lines are CLIENT-side (what the CLI requested and propagated), not
  49:   server-side attribution of what was served.
  50: - Unknown model: rejected loudly at the CLI layer before any API call
  51:   ("invalid model selection ... not recognized"), available models listed.
  52: - Write path: print mode cannot prompt, so unapproved tools soft-deny loudly
  53:   ("jetski: ... auto-denied", tool confirmation "Edit"/CodeAction).
  54:   `--mode accept-edits` does NOT apply in print mode. Without `--add-dir`,
  55:   writes divert to the CLI's internal scratch workspace
  56:   (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/scratch/`) — files never reach the real tree.
  57: - Working combination (WRITE4-OK): one-time interactive trust of the target
  58:   directory (persists to `trustedWorkspaces` in
  59:   `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`; the interactive session also set
  60:   `allowNonWorkspaceAccess: true`) + headless `--add-dir <dir>` binding →
  61:   file created in the bound tree, no prompting.
  62: - `agy --help` (v1.1.7) lists `--dangerously-skip-permissions` ("Auto-approve
  63:   all tool permission requests without prompting"). Whether it takes effect
  64:   in print mode is UNPROBED (accept-edits provably does not).
  65: - Environment note: an "Orca" app has pre-planted hooks in BOTH
  66:   `~/.gemini/settings.json` (gemini-cli hook events) and agy's stop hooks
  67:   (`jsonhook__orca-status_Stop_0_0` failed non-fatally in probe logs). Inert
  68:   outside Orca sessions; watch in drift triage if it starts failing loudly.

  81: ## Decisions (user, 2026-07-25)
  82: 
  83: - Architecture **A**: new `agents/flash-implementer.md`; `implementer.md`
  84:   kept as the direct-typing lane. Chosen over rewriting `implementer.md`
  85:   wholesale (B) after weighing the fable-advisor reference (which added its
  86:   vendor wrapper BESIDE the Claude lane), the untouched haiku transcription
  87:   lane, and the instant sonnet fallback against B's single-file tidiness.
  88:   C (controller shells the CLI directly) rejected: no isolation, no report
  89:   anchor. Known cost, accepted with mitigation: the two agent files share
  90:   contract sections that can drift; agent files cannot include each other,
  91:   so a byte-identity parity test is the only available enforcement and is
  92:   REQUIRED, not optional.
  93: - True-Flash-only, fails loudly: the wrapper never types repo code itself
  94:   and never completes work Flash failed. Enforced mechanically where
  95:   possible (tools allowlist, log/tree corroboration — see §1, §2), by
  96:   contract text where not.
  97: - Consent-gated reroute: a blocked task surfaces to the session AND the user;
  98:   rerouting to a Claude tier is an explicit user decision recorded in the
  99:   frozen plan's **Escalated points (user-decided)** section — the existing
 100:   artifact, not a new table. No automatic degradation — same ethos as the
 101:   debate lane's transport consent gate.
 102: - Scope: the Flash lane runs in the MAIN CHECKOUT ONLY this cycle. Trust is
 103:   per-directory and grantable only interactively, so fresh worktrees are
 104:   untrusted by construction; a worktree trust story is future-cycle work.
 105:   Coupling made explicit: with no disposable worktree, the
 106:   FILES-CHANGED-on-blocked rule (§4) is the recovery affordance for a
 107:   partially written tree.
 108: - Effort: ONE canonical variant this cycle, `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. No
 109:   per-task effort choice by any seat, no effort slot in the plan format —
 110:   a zero-judgment lane gets zero dispatch-time judgment calls.
 111: - **Sol check-off required before this branch closes**: plan debate before
 112:   implementation, diff debate before merge, both after the ~Jul 29 reset;
 113:   spec and plan drafting proceed now.
 114: 
 115: ## Design
 116: 
 117: ### 1. Agent: `agents/flash-implementer.md`
 118: 
 119: `model: haiku` frontmatter (wrapper/supervisor) plus an explicit `tools:`
 120: allowlist — Read, Grep, Glob, Bash; **no Edit, no Write, no NotebookEdit** —
 121: so the ergonomic path for the wrapper to type code itself does not exist.
 122: (Bash remains a write primitive in principle; the log/tree corroboration in
 123: §2 is the control that catches that path.) If the long-brief probe (§2)
 124: forces brief-via-file, the brief is written with a Bash heredoc — Write
 125: stays out of the allowlist.
 126: 
 127: Same input contract as `implementer.md` (ONE task's verbatim text + the
 128: plan's Global Constraints), same INPUT GAP rule. Report format gains one
 129: lane-specific line:
 130: 
 131: - **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <what is missing>
 132: - **ROUTE:** resolved model ID as requested-and-propagated, from the log,
 133:   plus the retained log file's path
 134: - **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on `blocked` this
 135:   MUST still list every path Flash already touched, so the session can
 136:   revert a partial write
 137: - **VERIFICATION:** each command run by the wrapper itself, with real output
 138: - **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" or an explanation of why the task could
 139:   not be built as written
 140: 
 141: The parity test (§6) pins the shared sections (INPUT GAP rule, report
 142: headings other than ROUTE) byte-identical across both agent files; ROUTE is
 143: declared lane-specific and excluded from parity.
 144: 
 145: ### 2. Dispatch recipe
 146: 
 147: - Preflight, all blocking, before any dispatch:
 148:   1. `agy models` output contains `gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (also proves
 149:      sign-in and binary presence);
 150:   2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` `trustedWorkspaces` contains
 151:      the workdir — if not, block with the exact re-trust instruction (one
 152:      interactive `agy` session in the workdir);
 153:   3. the same settings file carries NO per-tool allow rule targeting the
 154:      workdir (e.g. `write_file(...)`) — the durable, call-site-invisible
 155:      bypass class; this settings assertion is the LOAD-BEARING permission
 156:      control (a flag is at least visible per-dispatch; a settings rule
 157:      survives invisibly forever — tonight's probing itself left one behind).
 158: - Dispatch: `agy -p <brief> --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium
 159:   --add-dir <workdir> --log-file <fresh per-task log>`. Fresh log file per
 160:   attempt, written under the session scratchpad (never the repo tree — no
 161:   .gitignore dependency); each task's report carries its log path, and the
 162:   SDD execution record (the `.superpowers/sdd/<date>-<topic>/` workspace
 163:   the subagent-driven-development skill maintains) records per-task lane
 164:   provenance, which the diff-debate brief then carries. Adding an
 165:   implementer line to the frozen-plan debate record's Participants block is
 166:   declared scope for the plan debate to settle.
 167: - Route check, every run, on the log file:
 168:   1. `Print mode: starting` line present with `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`
 169:      (the RESOLVED ID is the comparand — same string §5 pins as canonical);
 170:   2. `Propagating selected model override` line present (presence-only; its
 171:      display label is a second namespace and is NOT matched);
 172:   3. log/tree corroboration: every path `git status` reports changed MUST
 173:      appear in the agy log as a file Flash touched — a changed file the log
 174:      never mentions means the wrapper (or something else) typed it, and the
 175:      task is `blocked` regardless of test results.
 176:   This evidence class is CLIENT-side: the check verifies the route was
 177:   **requested and propagated**, never "used and confirmed" (the doctor
 178:   check-4 language discipline, `commands/doctor.md`). Server-side
 179:   substitution is undetectable with today's evidence — named in Risks.
 180: - Probe task (pre-plan, throwaway harness, no agent file needed), pinned
 181:   before the agent body freezes:
 182:   1. long-brief mechanics — argument vs stdin vs file, quoting, size limits;
 183:   2. does agy emit ANY response-side model attribution (log tail, response
 184:      metadata; agy has no `--output-format` flag) — if yes, that artifact
 185:      becomes the route check and the client-side lines demote to
 186:      preconditions; the probe's answer is written into this section as the
 187:      final route-check contract text;
 188:   3. does `--dangerously-skip-permissions` take effect in print mode at all
 189:      (accept-edits provably does not) — if inert, the settings assertion is
 190:      the whole live defense, and §4's flag ban is belt-and-suspenders.
 191: 
 192: ### 3. One-time setup (documented in the agent file)
 193: 
 194: agy installed + signed in (done 2026-07-25). The repo root is trusted via
 195: one interactive agy session (same mechanism as the probe dir). An
 196: implementation task minimizes tonight's probe leftovers in agy's settings:
 197: remove the probe-dir `write_file` allow rule (required — §2's preflight
 198: asserts its absence for workdirs), and attempt the narrowest working
 199: `allowNonWorkspaceAccess` config with a DECLARED fallback: if narrowing
 200: does not converge quickly, leave the value as-is and document it and why —
 201: the task must not stall, and the setting is shared with the Orca app's
 202: environment. Trust is per-directory; the main-checkout-only scope decision
 203: above is the worktree story for this cycle.
 204: 
 205: ### 4. Failure handling — loud, never silent
 206: 
 207: `STATUS: blocked` with the exact output quoted, on: any preflight failure
 208: (sign-out, missing model, untrusted workdir, workdir allow rule present),
 209: the print-mode soft-deny line, nonzero exit, route-check failure (missing
 210: route lines, wrong resolved ID, log/tree corroboration mismatch), files
 211: diverted to internal scratch. Forbidden by class in the agent body: the
 212: `--dangerously-skip-permissions` flag by name, any `--mode`/approval bypass,
 213: AND persisting any per-tool allow rule for the workdir in agy settings —
 214: the durable settings-rule form is the load-bearing prohibition. On blocked,
 215: FILES CHANGED lists Flash's partial writes (per §1) as the recovery
 216: affordance. Reroute is consent-gated per Decisions.
 217: 
 218: ### 5. Model literal placement
 219: 
 220: The canonical implementer-lane literal is the RESOLVED ID
 221: `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`, living in `agents/flash-implementer.md` — the
 222: same string the preflight and route check compare against, so no
 223: requested/resolved ambiguity survives. After this cycle, no implementer
 224: model literal exists outside the two agent files. That claim is made true
 225: by enumerated edits, not assumption:
 226: 
 227: - `README.md` repo-map row (~line 66): "currently `model: sonnet`" literal
 228:   replaced by a pointer to the agent files; new row for the Flash lane.
 229: - `README.md` role-plug section (~lines 131-134): "`sonnet` today;
 230:   `haiku`/`opus` are drop-ins" reworded to point at the agent files without
 231:   naming tiers.
 232: - `agents/implementer.md` Lane note: gains the pointer to
 233:   `flash-implementer.md`; its "Nothing else in the plugin references the
 234:   implementer's model" sentence is RETIRED (already false today — README:66,
 235:   frozen-plan-format.md:4). Its own frontmatter/Lane-note literals stay:
 236:   agent files are the allowed homes.
 237: - `skills/multi-model-verify/references/frozen-plan-format.md` line 4:
 238:   "Sonnet 5" literal becomes a generic reference to the implementer agents.
 239: 
 240: Enforced by a new sweep test mirroring
 241: `test_reviewer_id_has_single_source`: scans the reviewer sweep's file set
 242: PLUS `agents/*.md`, with exactly the two agent files as the allowed homes —
 243: so a future third agent file is swept by default, not silently missed.
 244: 
 245: ### 6. Verification for this cycle
 246: 
 247: - Offline pytest (zero quota, both vendors): contract pins on the new agent
 248:   file — dispatch flags, resolved-ID comparand, route-check strings
 249:   (including the log/tree corroboration rule), forbidden-class phrasing,
 250:   report headings including ROUTE, `model: haiku` frontmatter, and the
 251:   `tools:` allowlist (no Edit/Write/NotebookEdit) — plus the §1 parity test
 252:   and the §5 implementer-literal sweep.
 253: - Pre-plan probes (throwaway harness, no agent file): the §2 probe task.
 254:   These are probes, not implementation — the plan-debate gate is not
 255:   crossed.
 256: - Live verification through the REAL agent happens during implementation,
 257:   AFTER the plan debate: one end-to-end dry-run task in a scratch repo
 258:   (trusted for the occasion), plus a red probe (bad model ID must produce
 259:   blocked, not output). Dev-loop ordering applies and is part of the task:
 260:   the agent is not live until `plugin.json` bumps,
 261:   `claude plugin update parallax@parallax` runs, and the session restarts.
 262: - Preflight cost: `agy models` is a network round-trip per task dispatch.
 263:   Deliberate — per-task freshness over quota thrift; the wrapper is fresh
 264:   per task, so there is no cheaper session-scoped cache to use.
 265: - Plan debate + diff debate (Sol): after the ~Jul 29 reset. The branch does
 266:   not merge without the diff debate's terminal PASS.
 267: - Behavioral battery: deliberately not run. Honest framing: this cycle
 268:   edits `references/frozen-plan-format.md`, a skill reference file that NO
 269:   behavioral case declares in its surface (and `agents/*` appears in no
 270:   surface either) — so `--changed` selects nothing by design, and the gap
 271:   itself is declared here for the debate to contest, alongside whether an
 272:   `agents/`-surfaced behavioral case (Flash-fails → wrapper reports
 273:   blocked, never substitutes) is wanted this cycle or deferred.
 274: - State-machine suite: `tools/check-drift.ps1` untouched → skipped
 275:   (CLAUDE.md opt-in rule).
 276: 
 277: ### 7. Scope edges
 278: 
 279: Version 0.12.0. Doctor gains a minimal agy row — binary version + pinned
 280: model present in `agy models`, no generation probe — and follows doctor's
 281: existing single-source discipline: the row PARSES the canonical ID out of
 282: `agents/flash-implementer.md` under the installed copy's `installPath`,
 283: carrying no literal of its own (the check-4 pattern). Drift-watch: the
 284: drift snapshot gains the `agy --version` string as an informational row

  38: - [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test file**
  39: 
  40: ```python
  41: """Contract pins for the Flash implementer lane (agents/flash-implementer.md).
  42: 
  43: Amended by design spec 2026-07-25 (advisory review B1-B8): these tests pin
  44: the agent file's contract text so drift in the dispatch recipe, route
  45: check, forbidden-bypass class, or report format fails offline with zero
  46: CLI calls. The two agent files are the only allowed homes for the
  47: implementer model literal.
  48: """
  49: import re
  50: from pathlib import Path
  51: 
  52: REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
  53: FLASH = REPO / "agents" / "flash-implementer.md"
  54: CLASSIC = REPO / "agents" / "implementer.md"
  55: CANONICAL_ID = "gemini-3.6-flash-medium"
  56: SHARED_START = "<!-- shared-contract:start -->"
  57: SHARED_END = "<!-- shared-contract:end -->"
  58: 
  59: 
  60: def _read(p):
  61:     return p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  62: 
  63: 
  64: def _frontmatter(text):
  65:     m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
  66:     assert m, "missing frontmatter"
  67:     return m.group(1)
  68: 
  69: 
  70: def test_flash_frontmatter_pins_model_and_tools():
  71:     fm = _frontmatter(_read(FLASH))
  72:     assert re.search(r"^model: haiku$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
  73:     m = re.search(r"^tools: (.+)$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
  74:     assert m, "tools allowlist missing"
  75:     tools = [t.strip() for t in m.group(1).split(",")]
  76:     assert sorted(tools) == ["Bash", "Glob", "Grep", "Read"]
  77: 
  78: 
  79: def test_flash_dispatch_contract():
  80:     body = _read(FLASH)
  81:     assert "--model " + CANONICAL_ID in body
  82:     assert "--add-dir" in body
  83:     assert "--log-file" in body
  84:     assert "AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md" in body
  85:     # stdin is probed-dead in print mode; the body must not suggest it
  86:     assert "stdin" not in body.lower() or "does not reach" in body.lower()
  87: 
  88: 
  89: def test_flash_route_check_strings():
  90:     body = _read(FLASH)
  91:     assert 'Print mode: starting' in body
  92:     assert 'model="' + CANONICAL_ID + '"' in body
  93:     assert "Propagating selected model override" in body
  94:     assert "requested and propagated" in body
  95:     assert "used and confirmed" not in body.replace(
  96:         'never "used and confirmed"', "")
  97:     # log/tree corroboration rule (advisory review B2 amendment)
  98:     assert "every path git status reports changed must appear in the agy log" in body.lower()
  99: 
 100: 
 101: def test_flash_preflight_pins():
 102:     body = _read(FLASH)
 103:     assert "agy models" in body
 104:     assert "trustedWorkspaces" in body
 105:     assert "allow rule" in body  # settings-rule absence assertion
 106: 
 107: 
 108: def test_flash_forbidden_bypass_class():
 109:     body = _read(FLASH)
 110:     assert "--dangerously-skip-permissions" in body
 111:     idx = body.find("--dangerously-skip-permissions")
 112:     window = body[max(0, idx - 200):idx + 200].lower()
 113:     assert "never" in window or "forbidden" in window
 114:     assert "persisted" in body and "settings" in body
 115: 
 116: 
 117: def test_flash_report_headings():
 118:     body = _read(FLASH)
 119:     for heading in ("**STATUS:**", "**ROUTE:**", "**FILES CHANGED:**",
 120:                     "**VERIFICATION:**", "**DEVIATIONS:**"):
 121:         assert heading in body, heading
 122:     # the four shared headings are pinned in BOTH files so a unilateral
 123:     # rename in implementer.md cannot pass the suite (ROUTE is
 124:     # lane-specific to the flash file)
 125:     classic = _read(CLASSIC)
 126:     for heading in ("**STATUS:**", "**FILES CHANGED:**",
 127:                     "**VERIFICATION:**", "**DEVIATIONS:**"):
 128:         assert heading in classic, heading
 129: 
 130: 
 131: def _shared_block(text, path):
 132:     assert SHARED_START in text and SHARED_END in text, (
 133:         "missing shared-contract markers in " + str(path))
 134:     return text.split(SHARED_START)[1].split(SHARED_END)[0]
 135: 
 136: 
 137: def test_shared_contract_parity():
 138:     # byte-identical shared block; ROUTE is lane-specific and lives
 139:     # outside the block (spec section 1)
 140:     assert _shared_block(_read(FLASH), FLASH) == _shared_block(
 141:         _read(CLASSIC), CLASSIC)
 142: 
 143: 
 144: def test_classic_lane_note_retired_stale_claim():
 145:     body = _read(CLASSIC)
 146:     assert "Nothing else in the plugin references" not in body
 147:     assert "flash-implementer" in body
 148: 
 149: 
 150: SWEEP_GLOBS = [
 151:     "skills/**/*.md", "commands/*.md", "tools/*.ps1", "hooks/*",
 152:     "evals/**/*.py", "evals/**/*.json", "evals/**/*.ps1",
 153:     "README.md", "CLAUDE.md", "agents/*.md",
 154: ]
 155: # The two agent files are the contract homes; this test file necessarily
 156: # carries the literal as its enforcement pin.
 157: ALLOWED = {FLASH.resolve(), CLASSIC.resolve(),
 158:            Path(__file__).resolve()}
 159: 
 160: 
 161: def test_flash_literal_single_source():
 162:     offenders = []
 163:     for pattern in SWEEP_GLOBS:
 164:         for p in REPO.glob(pattern):
 165:             if p.resolve() in ALLOWED:
 166:                 continue
 167:             if "gemini-3.6-flash" in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8",
 168:                                                  errors="replace"):
 169:                 offenders.append(str(p))
 170:     assert offenders == []
 171: 
 172: 
 173: def test_sonnet_implementer_literals_removed():
 174:     readme = _read(REPO / "README.md")
 175:     assert "currently `model: sonnet`" not in readme
 176:     # absence pins alone are vacuous against line-wrapped source; the
 177:     # presence pins below are the real oracles for Task 3's rewrites
 178:     assert "`haiku`/`opus` are drop-ins" not in readme
 179:     assert "any Claude tier is a drop-in" in readme
 180:     fpf = _read(REPO / "skills" / "multi-model-verify" / "references"
 181:                 / "frozen-plan-format.md")
 182:     assert "Sonnet 5" not in fpf
 183:     assert "the pinned lane in `agents/`" in fpf
 184: ```

 215: - [ ] **Step 1: Write `agents/flash-implementer.md` exactly as follows**
 216: 
 217: ````markdown
 218: ---
 219: name: flash-implementer
 220: description: Zero-judgment Flash implementer for frozen-plan tasks. Use when executing build tasks from a debate-frozen implementation plan - give it ONE task's verbatim text plus the plan's Global Constraints and a log-file path. It delegates ALL code-writing to Gemini 3.6 Flash via the Antigravity CLI headlessly, verifies route and authorship evidence, runs the task's verification itself, and reports. It never types repo code and never makes design decisions.
 221: model: haiku
 222: tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
 223: ---
 224: 
 225: # Flash implementer (agy wrapper)
 226: 
 227: You supervise ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. Gemini 3.6 Flash
 228: does ALL the typing through the Antigravity CLI (`agy`); you do preflight,
 229: dispatch, evidence checks, verification, and honest reporting. You never
 230: edit or create repo files yourself — your tool grant has no Edit or Write,
 231: and using Bash to write repo content is equally forbidden: a changed file
 232: the agy log cannot account for fails the task.
 233: 
 234: <!-- shared-contract:start -->
 235: ## The contract
 236: 
 237: - Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
 238:   the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
 239: - No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
 240:   adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
 241:   gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
 242: - **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
 243:   convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
 244:   path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
 245:   missing piece.
 246: - Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
 247:   claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
 248:   the task is not done.
 249: <!-- shared-contract:end -->
 250: 
 251: ## Inputs (from the dispatching controller)
 252: 
 253: - The task's verbatim text and the plan's Global Constraints.
 254: - The workspace directory (this cycle: the main checkout only).
 255: - A log-file path OUTSIDE the workspace (the controller owns it; you never
 256:   place logs in the repo tree).
 257: 
 258: ## Preflight (all three must pass BEFORE dispatch)
 259: 
 260: 1. `agy models` (binary at `$LOCALAPPDATA/agy/bin/agy.exe`) — output must
 261:    contain `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. Anything else (missing binary,
 262:    sign-out, missing model) is blocked.
 263: 2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — `trustedWorkspaces` must
 264:    contain the workspace directory. If not: blocked, and the report quotes
 265:    the fix ("run one interactive `agy` session in the workspace and approve
 266:    trust").
 267: 3. The same settings file must carry NO per-tool allow rule targeting the
 268:    workspace (for example `write_file(...)`). A persisted settings rule is
 269:    the durable, call-site-invisible bypass class — its absence is the
 270:    load-bearing permission control. If present: blocked, quoting the rule.
 271: 
 272: ## Dispatch
 273: 
 274: 1. Write the brief to `<workspace>/AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md` with a Bash heredoc:
 275:    the task's verbatim text, the Global Constraints, and the exact files
 276:    list. (stdin does not reach the model in print mode — probed 2026-07-25;
 277:    the workspace brief file is the delivery mechanism.)
 278: 2. Run (single line):
 279:    `agy -p "Read the file AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md in the workspace and execute it exactly." --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium --add-dir <workspace> --log-file <log-path>`
 280: 3. Delete `AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md` immediately after agy exits, BEFORE any
 281:    evidence check, so it never appears in `git status`.
 282: 
 283: ## Route and authorship checks (every run, on the log file)
 284: 
 285: - `Print mode: starting` line present containing
 286:   `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`.
 287: - `Propagating selected model override` line present (presence only — its
 288:   display label is not matched).
 289: - Log/tree corroboration: every path git status reports changed must
 290:   appear in the agy log as a file Flash touched. A changed file the log
 291:   never mentions means someone other than Flash typed it — blocked, no
 292:   matter what the tests say.
 293: - This evidence is client-side: report the route as **requested and
 294:   propagated**, never "used and confirmed". Server-side substitution is
 295:   not detectable from this evidence class.
 296: 
 297: ## Failure handling — loud, never silent
 298: 
 299: Blocked (quote the exact output) on: any preflight failure, the print-mode
 300: soft-deny line ("auto-denied"), nonzero exit, a missing or mismatched
 301: route line, a corroboration mismatch, or writes diverted to agy's internal
 302: scratch (expected files absent from the tree). Never retry with
 303: `--dangerously-skip-permissions` — that flag is forbidden in this lane, as
 304: is ANY approval-bypass flag or persisted per-tool allow rule added to agy
 305: settings. Never complete the work yourself: rerouting a blocked task to a
 306: Claude tier is the user's decision, recorded in the plan's Escalated
 307: points — not yours.
 308: 
 309: ## Report format (your final message)
 310: 
 311: - **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <exactly what is missing>
 312: - **ROUTE:** the resolved model ID as requested and propagated, plus the
 313:   retained log file's path
 314: - **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on blocked, STILL
 315:   list every path Flash already touched so the session can revert a
 316:   partial write
 317: - **VERIFICATION:** each command you ran yourself, with its real output
 318:   (condensed)
 319: - **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" — anything else means you stopped and are
 320:   explaining why the task could not be built as written
 321: 
 322: ## Lane note
 323: 
 324: This agent pins the Flash implementation lane. Canonical model literal:
 325: `gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (Gemini 3.6 Flash, medium reasoning effort,
 326: Antigravity CLI resolved ID). The literal lives ONLY here;
 327: `implementer.md` pins its own lane's model in its frontmatter and Lane
 328: note — every other surface points at the agent files. Trust is per-directory and interactive-only, so this lane runs in
 329: the main checkout this cycle — a worktree trust story is future work.
 330: ````

===== docs\superpowers\plans\rounds\2026-07-25-flash-implementer\plan-round1-brief.md =====
   1: # Plan-stage review brief: parallax 0.12.0 Flash implementer lane
   2: 
   3: ## Your role
   4: 
   5: You are the cross-vendor reviewer in a two-party verification debate (Kimi
   6: K3, reviewing work authored by a Claude-family session). This is the plan
   7: gate: the design spec and implementation plan are under review BEFORE any
   8: implementation. You are the backup reviewer lane; the primary reviewer's
   9: transport (codex) is quota-exhausted, which is this lane's designed
  10: trigger. Your verdict feeds a recorded debate; a separate primary-reviewer
  11: check-off will still occur before the branch merges.
  12: 
  13: ## Subject under review (read both, fully)
  14: 
  15: 1. docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-flash-implementer-design.md (the
  16:    approved design spec, including its probe record and amendments)
  17: 2. docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-25-flash-implementer.md (the
  18:    implementation plan candidate — the primary subject: does it correctly
  19:    and completely implement the spec, with defect-free tasks?)
  20: 
  21: ## Context files you should consult for grounding
  22: 
  23: - agents/implementer.md (the existing agent the new lane sits beside)
  24: - README.md (repo map ~line 66; role-plug section ~lines 126-139)
  25: - skills/multi-model-verify/references/frozen-plan-format.md (line 4)
  26: - commands/doctor.md (the doctor pattern Task 4 extends)
  27: - evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py (the test
  28:   conventions the new test file imitates)
  29: - CLAUDE.md (project verification commands and conventions)
  30: 
  31: ## Rules of engagement
  32: 
  33: - Evidence-grounded: every finding cites file:line from THIS workspace.
  34:   You have read-only tools (ReadFile, Glob, Grep) by design — you cannot
  35:   run commands; do not report the inability to execute as a finding.
  36: - Externally probed facts stated in the spec's probe record (agy CLI
  37:   behavior, model lists, log line formats) are GIVEN — you cannot verify
  38:   them offline; do not contest them as unverified. Contest instead:
  39:   internal contradictions, spec-to-plan coverage gaps, defective task
  40:   steps (wrong paths, wrong pinned strings, tests that cannot pass or
  41:   that pass vacuously), missing verification, and unstated failure modes.
  42: - A claim you cannot support with file:line evidence must be labeled
  43:   UNVERIFIED, not asserted.
  44: - No manufactured objections: grade final dispositions. A trivial
  45:   amendment you propose and would accept is not a blocking finding.
  46:   Style preferences are not findings.
  47: - Verdict grammar: PASS (plan-ready, possibly "PASS with trivial
  48:   amendments" listing them) | FIX (specific blocking defects, each with
  49:   evidence and a concrete proposed change) | ESCALATE (a decision only
  50:   the user can make; state the decision needed).
  51: 
  52: ## Output format (exactly this structure)
  53: 
  54: 1. FINDINGS — numbered; each: severity (BLOCKING | MINOR), file:line,
  55:    the defect, the concrete fix.
  56: 2. COVERAGE CHECK — one line per spec design section (1-7): covered by
  57:    which plan task, or GAP.
  58: 3. VERDICT — PASS / FIX / ESCALATE with one-paragraph justification.

   1: # Cross-vendor plan-gate review — parallax 0.12.0 Flash implementer lane
   2: 
   3: Reviewer: Kimi K3 (backup lane; primary transport quota-exhausted). Subjects: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-flash-implementer-design.md` and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-25-flash-implementer.md`, read in full, grounded against the workspace files named in the brief. Probed facts in the spec's probe record and the plan's Global Constraints (agy CLI behavior, log line formats, model lists) are treated as GIVEN per the rules of engagement.
   4: 
   5: ## 1. FINDINGS
   6: 
   7: **1. BLOCKING — plan:433 vs plan:144-147,160 — Task 4's doctor text trips the plan's own literal sweep, making Task 4 Step 4's expected green unreachable.**
   8: Task 4 Step 1 appends doctor check 7 containing "parse the canonical model literal from its Lane note (the `gemini-3.6-flash-*` token)" (plan:433). The Task 1 sweep test scans `commands/*.md` (plan:144) and flags any file containing the substring `"gemini-3.6-flash"` (plan:160) outside the ALLOWED set (plan:150-151, which excludes doctor.md). The wildcard form `gemini-3.6-flash-*` contains that substring, so after Task 4 Step 1, `test_flash_literal_single_source` FAILS — yet Task 4 Step 4 expects `python -m pytest evals -q` green (plan:469-470). It also violates the spec's single-source discipline for this row ("carrying no literal of its own (the check-4 pattern)", spec:280-283).
   9: **Fix:** reword the doctor text to not name the model family, e.g. "parse the canonical model literal from its Lane note (the pinned model-ID token declared there) — the agent file is the ONE place the implementer model is defined; carry no literal here." (Alternative: change the sweep to match full variant IDs `gemini-3.6-flash-(low|medium|high)`, which the wildcard text would no longer match — but the reword is smaller and keeps the sweep's prefix catch-all.)
  10: 
  11: **2. MINOR — plan:178 vs plan:130-134 — Task 1 Step 2's expected failure reason for the parity test is wrong.**
  12: `test_shared_contract_parity` calls `_shared_block(_read(FLASH), FLASH)` (plan:133); with the agent file absent, `_read(FLASH)` raises `FileNotFoundError` before the marker assertion can fire, so the failure signature is FileNotFoundError, not "missing shared-contract markers" as plan:178 states. A zero-judgment executor comparing actual vs expected output sees a mismatch on a step that is actually behaving correctly.
  13: **Fix:** amend plan:178 to read: parity fails with FileNotFoundError at this stage; the "missing shared-contract markers" assertion only appears once the file exists without markers.
  14: 
  15: **3. MINOR — plan:168-169 vs README.md:131-134 — the README role-plug absence pin passes vacuously; Task 3 Step 2 is verified by no test.**
  16: The pin asserts `"`haiku`/`opus` are drop-ins" not in readme` (plan:169), but the current README wraps the phrase across a line break: "(`sonnet` today; `haiku`/`opus` are\n  drop-ins)" (README.md:132-133). The contiguous string is absent today, so the pin passes at baseline AND passes if Task 3 Step 2 is skipped entirely — the rewrite (plan:383-385) has no test oracle.
  17: **Fix:** add a presence pin for the replacement, e.g. `assert "any Claude tier is a drop-in" in readme`, or normalize whitespace in the readme before matching.
  18: 
  19: **4. MINOR — plan:144-147 vs test_multi_model_verify.py:248-250 and spec:240-243 — the new sweep drops `evals/**/*.ps1` from the file set it claims to mirror.**
  20: Spec §5 requires "the reviewer sweep's file set PLUS `agents/*.md`" (spec:240-243). The reviewer sweep's set includes `"evals/**/*.ps1"` (test_multi_model_verify.py:249); the plan's SWEEP_GLOBS (plan:144-147) omits it while adding `agents/*.md`. A future `gemini-3.6-flash` literal in an evals .ps1 (e.g. drift_statemachine_tests.ps1) would be silently missed.
  21: **Fix:** add `"evals/**/*.ps1"` to SWEEP_GLOBS.
  22: 
  23: **5. MINOR — plan:315-316 vs plan:334-344 — the flash agent's Lane note makes a false claim about where the canonical literal lives.**
  24: The pinned agent text says "The literal lives ONLY here and in `implementer.md`'s Lane note" (plan:315-316), but Task 2 Step 2's replacement Lane-note text for implementer.md (plan:339-343) contains no gemini literal — implementer.md pins "currently Sonnet 5" for its own lane. The claim misdirects a future lane-swapper. (The sweep's ALLOWED set, plan:150-151, tolerates both files, so no test catches this.)
  25: **Fix:** reword to "The literal lives ONLY here; `implementer.md` pins its own lane's model in its frontmatter and Lane note — every other surface points at the agent files."
  26: 
  27: **6. MINOR — plan:454-457 vs tools/check-drift.ps1:193-205,274-279 — the snapshot snippet contradicts the file's mandated style and its no-clobber invariant; one aspect UNVERIFIED.**
  28: The plan instructs adding `agy = if (Test-Path ...) { ... } else { "N/A" }` "adjacent and in the same style" as the codex property. But the file's actual style precomputes carry-forward variables (`$codexVersionToSave`, check-drift.ps1:199-205) precisely so a transient probe failure never clobbers the last known-good value — an invariant the file's own comments call a quiet path (check-drift.ps1:193-197). The `"N/A"` fallback violates it. Additionally, UNVERIFIED: an `if` statement used directly as a hashtable value is likely a parse error in Windows PowerShell 5.1, the script's mandated runtime (check-drift.ps1:21-23); the state-machine suite (Task 4 Step 3) would adjudicate, but the plan should not hand the executor a snippet that likely fails against its own expected output.
  29: **Fix:** compute `$agyVersion` beside lines 119-125, add `$agyVersionToSave` with `$snapshot.agy` carry-forward beside lines 198-205, and write `agy = $agyVersionToSave` in `$newSnapshot` (check-drift.ps1:274-279).
  30: 
  31: **7. MINOR — plan:573-576 vs plan:268 and spec:108-110 — the red probe's mechanism contradicts the lane's zero-judgment contract.**
  32: Task 6 Step 4 expects the wrapper to dispatch with `--model gemini-9.9-fake` because "the controller's brief pins" it. But the agent body hardcodes `--model gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (plan:268), and the design forbids per-task model/effort choice by any seat (spec:108-110). A contract-faithful wrapper may refuse the contradictory brief (blocked/INPUT GAP) rather than dispatch — a correct behavior that the step's single expected outcome ("blocked quoting agy's 'invalid model selection' output", plan:575-576) would read as failure.
  33: **Fix:** make the expectation disjunctive: PASS = STATUS blocked with no file changes, whether by wrapper refusal of the contradictory brief or by agy's CLI-level rejection; FAIL = any file change or STATUS done.
  34:

  36: The instruction names the entry `write_file(/Users/Brandon/AppData/.../agy-probe/)` — a hybrid `/Users/.../AppData` spelling. The settings file is outside this workspace and agy's path normalization is not offline-verifiable, so the exact string is UNVERIFIED; a literal-match instruction may stall a zero-judgment executor if agy spelled it differently.
  37: **Fix:** instruct deleting "the `write_file` allow entry in `permissions.allow` whose path targets the agy-probe directory, whatever its exact spelling", verified by re-reading the file after the edit.
  38:

  40: Spec §1: "The parity test (§6) pins the shared sections (INPUT GAP rule, report headings other than ROUTE) byte-identical across both agent files" (spec:141-143). The plan's parity block covers only the contract section (which carries the INPUT GAP rule) (plan:223-238); `test_flash_report_headings` pins heading presence in the flash file only (plan:117-121). A unilateral heading edit in implementer.md (e.g. renaming `**FILES CHANGED:**`) passes the whole suite.
  41: **Fix:** extend parity to the report headings — e.g. a second marked block around the four shared heading lines, or an assertion that the shared heading lines extracted from CLASSIC appear verbatim in FLASH.
