===== agents\implementer.md =====
   1: ---
   2: name: implementer
   3: description: Zero-judgment implementer for frozen-plan tasks. Use when executing tasks from a debate-frozen implementation plan - give it ONE task's verbatim text plus the plan's Global Constraints. It builds exactly what the spec says, runs the spec's verification, and reports evidence. It never makes design decisions.
   4: model: sonnet
   5: ---
   6: 
   7: # Implementer
   8: 
   9: You execute ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. The plan was already
  10: verified by a cross-model debate — the spec carries all the judgment; you
  11: carry none.
  12: 
  13: <!-- shared-contract:start -->
  14: ## The contract
  15: 
  16: - Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
  17:   the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
  18: - No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
  19:   adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
  20:   gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
  21: - **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
  22:   convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
  23:   path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
  24:   missing piece.
  25: - Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
  26:   claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
  27:   the task is not done.
  28: <!-- shared-contract:end -->
  29: 
  30: ## Report format (your final message)
  31: 
  32: - **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <exactly what is missing>
  33: - **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths, from the diff
  34: - **VERIFICATION:** each command run, with its real output (condensed)
  35: - **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" — anything else means you stopped and are
  36:   explaining why the task could not be built as written
  37: 
  38: ## Lane note
  39: 
  40: This agent pins the direct-typing Claude lane (currently Sonnet 5;
  41: transcription tasks dispatch it with a haiku override). Build tasks run on
  42: the Flash lane instead — see `flash-implementer.md`, the supervisor-pattern
  43: wrapper this note's vendor-swap path describes. Two swap paths:
  44: 
  45: - **Another Claude tier** (sonnet/haiku/opus): edit the `model:` line in
  46:   this file's frontmatter — done.
  47: - **Another vendor's model** (a Grok or Codex lane, fable-advisor style):
  48:   the `model:` frontmatter only takes Claude models, so keep a cheap Claude
  49:   tier here as the SUPERVISOR and change the body to delegate the typing to
  50:   that vendor's CLI — spec to a temp file, pipe to the CLI in a
  51:   workspace-write sandbox, then re-run the task's verification yourself
  52:   before reporting (never trust the external model's completion claim).
  53:   The report format above stays the contract either way.
===== agents\flash-implementer.md =====
   1: ---
   2: name: flash-implementer
   3: description: Zero-judgment Flash implementer for frozen-plan tasks. Use when executing build tasks from a debate-frozen implementation plan - give it ONE task's verbatim text plus the plan's Global Constraints and a log-file path. It delegates ALL code-writing to Gemini 3.6 Flash via the Antigravity CLI headlessly, verifies route and authorship evidence, runs the task's verification itself, and reports. It never types repo code and never makes design decisions.
   4: model: haiku
   5: tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
   6: ---
   7: 
   8: # Flash implementer (agy wrapper)
   9: 
  10: You supervise ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. Gemini 3.6 Flash
  11: does ALL the typing through the Antigravity CLI (`agy`); you do preflight,
  12: dispatch, evidence checks, verification, and honest reporting. You never
  13: edit or create repo files yourself — your tool grant has no Edit or Write,
  14: and using Bash to write repo content is equally forbidden: a changed file
  15: the agy log cannot account for fails the task.
  16: 
  17: <!-- shared-contract:start -->
  18: ## The contract
  19: 
  20: - Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
  21:   the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
  22: - No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
  23:   adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
  24:   gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
  25: - **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
  26:   convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
  27:   path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
  28:   missing piece.
  29: - Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
  30:   claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
  31:   the task is not done.
  32: <!-- shared-contract:end -->
  33: 
  34: ## Inputs (from the dispatching controller)
  35: 
  36: - The task's verbatim text and the plan's Global Constraints.
  37: - The workspace directory (this cycle: the main checkout only).
  38: - A log-file path OUTSIDE the workspace (the controller owns it; you never
  39:   place logs in the repo tree).
  40: 
  41: ## Preflight (all three must pass BEFORE dispatch)
  42: 
  43: 1. `agy models` (binary at `$LOCALAPPDATA/agy/bin/agy.exe`) — output must
  44:    contain `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. Anything else (missing binary,
  45:    sign-out, missing model) is blocked.
  46: 2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — `trustedWorkspaces` must
  47:    contain the workspace directory. If not: blocked, and the report quotes
  48:    the fix ("run one interactive `agy` session in the workspace and approve
  49:    trust").
  50: 3. The same settings file must carry NO per-tool allow rule targeting the
  51:    workspace (for example `write_file(...)`). A persisted settings rule is
  52:    the durable, call-site-invisible bypass class — its absence is the
  53:    load-bearing permission control. If present: blocked, quoting the rule.
  54: 
  55: ## Dispatch
  56: 
  57: 1. Write the brief to `<workspace>/AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md` with a Bash heredoc:
  58:    the task's verbatim text, the Global Constraints, and the exact files
  59:    list. (stdin does not reach the model in print mode — probed 2026-07-25;
  60:    the workspace brief file is the delivery mechanism.)
  61: 2. Run (single line):
  62:    `agy -p "Read the file AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md in the workspace and execute it exactly." --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium --add-dir <workspace> --log-file <log-path>`
  63: 3. Delete `AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md` immediately after agy exits, BEFORE any
  64:    evidence check, so it never appears in `git status`.
  65: 
  66: ## Route and authorship checks (every run, on the log file)
  67: 
  68: - `Print mode: starting` line present containing
  69:   `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`.
  70: - `Propagating selected model override` line present (presence only — its
  71:   display label is not matched).
  72: - Log/tree corroboration: every path git status reports changed must appear in the agy log as a file Flash touched. A changed file the log never mentions means someone other than Flash typed it — blocked, no matter what the tests say.
  73: - This evidence is client-side: report the route as **requested and
  74:   propagated**, never "used and confirmed". Server-side substitution is
  75:   not detectable from this evidence class.
  76: 
  77: ## Failure handling — loud, never silent
  78: 
  79: Blocked (quote the exact output) on: any preflight failure, the print-mode
  80: soft-deny line ("auto-denied"), nonzero exit, a missing or mismatched
  81: route line, a corroboration mismatch, or writes diverted to agy's internal
  82: scratch (expected files absent from the tree). Never retry with
  83: `--dangerously-skip-permissions` — that flag is forbidden in this lane, as
  84: is ANY approval-bypass flag or persisted per-tool allow rule added to agy
  85: settings. Never complete the work yourself: rerouting a blocked task to a
  86: Claude tier is the user's decision, recorded in the plan's Escalated
  87: points — not yours.
  88: 
  89: ## Report format (your final message)
  90: 
  91: - **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <exactly what is missing>
  92: - **ROUTE:** the resolved model ID as requested and propagated, plus the
  93:   retained log file's path
  94: - **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on blocked, STILL
  95:   list every path Flash already touched so the session can revert a
  96:   partial write
  97: - **VERIFICATION:** each command you ran yourself, with its real output
  98:   (condensed)
  99: - **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" — anything else means you stopped and are
 100:   explaining why the task could not be built as written
 101: 
 102: ## Lane note
 103: 
 104: This agent pins the Flash implementation lane. Canonical model literal:
 105: `gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (Gemini 3.6 Flash, medium reasoning effort,
 106: Antigravity CLI resolved ID). The literal lives ONLY here;
 107: `implementer.md` pins its own lane's model in its frontmatter and Lane
 108: note — every other surface points at the agent files. Trust is per-directory and interactive-only, so this lane runs in
 109: the main checkout this cycle — a worktree trust story is future work.
===== evals\multi-model-verify\test_flash_implementer.py =====
   1: """Contract pins for the Flash implementer lane (agents/flash-implementer.md).
   2: 
   3: Amended by design spec 2026-07-25 (advisory review B1-B8): these tests pin
   4: the agent file's contract text so drift in the dispatch recipe, route
   5: check, forbidden-bypass class, or report format fails offline with zero
   6: CLI calls. The two agent files are the only allowed homes for the
   7: implementer model literal.
   8: """
   9: import re
  10: from pathlib import Path
  11: 
  12: REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
  13: FLASH = REPO / "agents" / "flash-implementer.md"
  14: CLASSIC = REPO / "agents" / "implementer.md"
  15: CANONICAL_ID = "gemini-3.6-flash-medium"
  16: SHARED_START = "<!-- shared-contract:start -->"
  17: SHARED_END = "<!-- shared-contract:end -->"
  18: 
  19: 
  20: def _read(p):
  21:     return p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  22: 
  23: 
  24: def _frontmatter(text):
  25:     m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
  26:     assert m, "missing frontmatter"
  27:     return m.group(1)
  28: 
  29: 
  30: def test_flash_frontmatter_pins_model_and_tools():
  31:     fm = _frontmatter(_read(FLASH))
  32:     assert re.search(r"^model: haiku$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
  33:     m = re.search(r"^tools: (.+)$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
  34:     assert m, "tools allowlist missing"
  35:     tools = [t.strip() for t in m.group(1).split(",")]
  36:     assert sorted(tools) == ["Bash", "Glob", "Grep", "Read"]
  37: 
  38: 
  39: def test_flash_dispatch_contract():
  40:     body = _read(FLASH)
  41:     assert "--model " + CANONICAL_ID in body
  42:     assert "--add-dir" in body
  43:     assert "--log-file" in body
  44:     assert "AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md" in body
  45:     # stdin is probed-dead in print mode; the body must not suggest it
  46:     assert "stdin" not in body.lower() or "does not reach" in body.lower()
  47: 
  48: 
  49: def test_flash_route_check_strings():
  50:     body = _read(FLASH)
  51:     assert 'Print mode: starting' in body
  52:     assert 'model="' + CANONICAL_ID + '"' in body
  53:     assert "Propagating selected model override" in body
  54:     assert "requested and propagated" in body
  55:     assert "used and confirmed" not in body.replace(
  56:         'never "used and confirmed"', "")
  57:     # log/tree corroboration rule (advisory review B2 amendment)
  58:     assert "every path git status reports changed must appear in the agy log" in body.lower()
  59: 
  60: 
  61: def test_flash_preflight_pins():
  62:     body = _read(FLASH)
  63:     assert "agy models" in body
  64:     assert "trustedWorkspaces" in body
  65:     assert "allow rule" in body  # settings-rule absence assertion
  66: 
  67: 
  68: def test_flash_forbidden_bypass_class():
  69:     body = _read(FLASH)
  70:     assert "--dangerously-skip-permissions" in body
  71:     idx = body.find("--dangerously-skip-permissions")
  72:     window = body[max(0, idx - 200):idx + 200].lower()
  73:     assert "never" in window or "forbidden" in window
  74:     assert "persisted" in body and "settings" in body
  75: 
  76: 
  77: def test_flash_report_headings():
  78:     body = _read(FLASH)
  79:     for heading in ("**STATUS:**", "**ROUTE:**", "**FILES CHANGED:**",
  80:                     "**VERIFICATION:**", "**DEVIATIONS:**"):
  81:         assert heading in body, heading
  82:     # the four shared headings are pinned in BOTH files so a unilateral
  83:     # rename in implementer.md cannot pass the suite (ROUTE is
  84:     # lane-specific to the flash file)
  85:     classic = _read(CLASSIC)
  86:     for heading in ("**STATUS:**", "**FILES CHANGED:**",
  87:                     "**VERIFICATION:**", "**DEVIATIONS:**"):
  88:         assert heading in classic, heading
  89: 
  90: 
  91: def _shared_block(text, path):
  92:     assert SHARED_START in text and SHARED_END in text, (
  93:         "missing shared-contract markers in " + str(path))
  94:     return text.split(SHARED_START)[1].split(SHARED_END)[0]
  95: 
  96: 
  97: def test_shared_contract_parity():
  98:     # byte-identical shared block; ROUTE is lane-specific and lives
  99:     # outside the block (spec section 1)
 100:     assert _shared_block(_read(FLASH), FLASH) == _shared_block(
 101:         _read(CLASSIC), CLASSIC)
 102: 
 103: 
 104: def test_classic_lane_note_retired_stale_claim():
 105:     body = _read(CLASSIC)
 106:     assert "Nothing else in the plugin references" not in body
 107:     assert "flash-implementer" in body
 108: 
 109: 
 110: SWEEP_GLOBS = [
 111:     "skills/**/*.md", "commands/*.md", "tools/*.ps1", "hooks/*",
 112:     "evals/**/*.py", "evals/**/*.json", "evals/**/*.ps1",
 113:     "README.md", "CLAUDE.md", "agents/*.md",
 114: ]
 115: # The two agent files are the contract homes; this test file necessarily
 116: # carries the literal as its enforcement pin.
 117: ALLOWED = {FLASH.resolve(), CLASSIC.resolve(),
 118:            Path(__file__).resolve()}
 119: 
 120: 
 121: def test_flash_literal_single_source():
 122:     offenders = []
 123:     for pattern in SWEEP_GLOBS:
 124:         for p in REPO.glob(pattern):
 125:             if p.resolve() in ALLOWED:
 126:                 continue
 127:             if "gemini-3.6-flash" in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8",
 128:                                                  errors="replace"):
 129:                 offenders.append(str(p))

 135:     assert "currently `model: sonnet`" not in readme
 136:     # absence pins alone are vacuous against line-wrapped source; the
 137:     # presence pins below are the real oracles for Task 3's rewrites
 138:     assert "`haiku`/`opus` are drop-ins" not in readme

===== README.md =====
   1: # parallax
   2: 
   3: **Cross-model verification for Claude Code.** Two equal-weight frontier
   4: models — the session model and a cross-vendor reviewer (via the OpenAI
   5: codex CLI; the current reviewer lane is declared in the skill's
   6: model-prompting-notes.md) —
   7: verify and refute each other's claims with file:line evidence *before* a
   8: cheaper implementer touches code, and again *before* the result merges.
   9: Neither vendor grades its own homework.
  10: 
  11: Companion to [superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers), not a
  12: replacement: it fills the cross-model review gap superpowers rules out of
  13: scope.
  14: 
  15: ## How it works
  16: 
  17: ```mermaid
  18: flowchart LR
  19:     A[superpowers<br/>brainstorm] --> B{{"mode plan debate<br/>session ⇄ reviewer"}}
  20:     B -->|converged| C["frozen plan<br/>+ debate record"]
  21:     B -->|escalated| U[user decides]
  22:     C --> D["implementer subagent<br/>(zero judgment calls)"]
  23:     D --> E{{"mode diff debate<br/>spec + port fidelity"}}
  24:     E -->|PASS| F([merge])
  25:     E -->|FIX| D
  26:     E -->|ESCALATE| U
  27: ```
  28: 
  29: - **Mode `plan`** — after brainstorming, before the implementation plan is
  30:   written. The two models debate the approach, port-fidelity claims, and the
  31:   API/behavior risk register until convergence or the round cap, then the
  32:   converged plan is frozen with a full debate record
  33:   (participants, rounds, resolved/struck/escalated points, verification
  34:   status).
  35: - **Mode `diff`** — after implementation, alongside superpowers code review.
  36:   A PostToolUse hook fingerprints the superpowers code-reviewer dispatch and
  37:   injects the diff-mode reminder with the same base/head SHAs, so both
  38:   reviews always look at the same range. Verdicts are PASS / FIX / ESCALATE
  39:   from *each* side.
  40: 
  41: The debate rules that keep this honest
  42: (`skills/multi-model-verify/references/debate-protocol.md`):
  43: 
  44: - **Strike rule** — every externally checkable claim carries a citation the
  45:   other side can read (`References/<addon>/<file>:<line>`, API docs, a dated
  46:   probe). Uncited claims are struck, not debated.
  47: - **Equal weight** — disagreements resolve by evidence, never by which model
  48:   said it. Unresolvable points escalate to the user with both positions.
  49: - **Round cap** — 4 exchanges by default; convergence in one round on a
  50:   sound plan is the system working, and manufactured objections are a
  51:   protocol violation, not diligence.
  52: - **Session continuity** — the reviewer keeps debate state across rounds via
  53:   `codex exec … resume <SESSION_ID>`; the full context is sent once.
  54: - **Final adjudication** — the session always has the last step: it
  55:   verifies the reviewer's final round against the repo and emits the
  56:   terminal verdict itself. A reviewer PASS/FIX is input, never the
  57:   decision; genuine deadlocks escalate to the user. The rule attaches to
  58:   the *role*, not the model — it holds whoever fills the session seat.
  59: 
  60: ## What's in the box
  61: 
  62: | Piece | What it does |
  63: |---|---|
  64: | `skills/multi-model-verify/` | The debate skill: both modes, debate protocol, frozen-plan format, model prompting notes, fallbacks/consent gate |
  65: | `hooks/` | PostToolUse + PostToolUseFailure hook (matcher `Task\|Agent`): fingerprints the superpowers code-reviewer dispatch, injects the mode-`diff` reminder with matching SHAs; inert everywhere else |
  66: | `agents/implementer.md` | Zero-judgment direct-typing executor for frozen-plan tasks (model pinned in the file's frontmatter) |
  67: | `agents/flash-implementer.md` | Zero-judgment Flash lane: haiku wrapper drives Gemini Flash through the Antigravity CLI headlessly; route + authorship evidence checked every run (model literal pinned in the file) |
  68: | `commands/drift-triage.md` | `/parallax:drift-triage` — reads the newest drift report, verifies each finding against the live contract surfaces, repairs on a branch |
  69: | `commands/doctor.md` | `/parallax:doctor` — operational health check: checkout-vs-installed version, hook registration, superpowers fingerprint, codex transport round-trip, drift task + pending entries. Reports, never fixes |
  70: | `commands/intake.md` | `/parallax:intake` — external-reference intake: clone read-only as untrusted subject data, ground every claimed delta on both sides, probe-gate behavior claims, rank dispositions for the user's scope pick, then hand into the multi-model-verify debate |
  71: | `evals/` | Four gate tiers for the skill itself — see [Verify](#verify) |
  72: | `tools/check-drift.ps1` | Weekly drift watch over the three upstreams the contract depends on — see [Drift protection](#drift-protection) |
  73: | `tools/write-attestation.ps1` · `tools/verify-attestation.ps1` | SHA-bound review attestations — see [Attestation lane](#attestation-lane) |
  74: | `.githooks/pre-push` | Non-blocking attestation check on `main` pushes (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks` to enable) |
  75: 
  76: ## Fails loud, never silent
  77: 
  78: The governing rule
  79: (`skills/multi-model-verify/references/fallbacks.md`): **no transition that reduces
  80: vendor diversity, evidence quality, or conversation continuity happens
  81: without explicit user consent.**
  82: 
  83: ```mermaid
  84: flowchart TD
  85:     X[codex failure] --> R{one same-parameters retry}
  86:     R -->|recovered| OK[debate continues]
  87:     R -->|still failing| G["CONSENT GATE banner:<br/>what failed · what degraded mode<br/>would and would NOT verify"]
  88:     X -. "quota-exhausted:<br/>skip the retry" .-> G
  89:     X -. "missing-rollout resume:<br/>skip the retry" .-> G
  90:     G -->|fix codex| OK
  91:     G -->|run degraded| D["single-vendor, visibly flagged;<br/>DEGRADED plan poisons any diff PASS"]
  92:     G -->|abort or unattended| B2[BLOCKED / DEGRADED-NOT-AUTHORIZED]
  93: ```
  94: 
  95: - One same-parameters retry is the **only** automatic recovery; every other
  96:   transition stops at the consent gate. Unattended runs fail closed.
  97:   (Pre-dispatch failures retry free; a post-dispatch retry is the bounded
  98:   re-spend — never a loop.)
  99: - Session/weekly usage limits get a dedicated `quota-exhausted` class: no
 100:   retry (quota windows don't clear in seconds), and the banner quotes
 101:   codex's reset time verbatim. A resume failing with the missing-rollout
 102:   signature ("no rollout found ... code -32600", probed 2026-07-24) also
 103:   skips the retry — the rollout is gone; straight to the session-loss
 104:   consent gate.
 105: - **Effective-route check (0.6.0; `sandbox:` line added in 0.8.0)**: every
 106:   codex call's startup header
 107:   (`model:` / `provider:` / `reasoning effort:` / `sandbox: read-only`,
 108:   plus the `session id:` on
 109:   resume) is verified against the canonical declarations — a config.toml
 110:   override or profile silently swapping the reviewer is a named
 111:   `route-mismatch` class: no retry, reply discarded, consent gate.
 112:   (Sandbox mode has no continuity across resumes — an omitted flag falls
 113:   back to the config default, probed 2026-07-24.) The
 114:   call itself runs with reroute-capable env vars stripped
 115:   (`CODEX_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `CODEX_HOME`) after a
 116:   `codex login status` preflight that requires the first-party ChatGPT
 117:   state, not merely exit 0.
 118: - A DEGRADED-frozen plan cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS: mode `diff`
 119:   must first retrospectively cross-verify the plan's claims, and if
 120:   cross-vendor is *still* unavailable the only terminal state is
 121:   `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`.
 122: - Missing reference material for a port is a **hard stop** (ask the user),
 123:   never a degraded mode — a debate about remembered code is two models
 124:   fabricating at each other.
 125: 
 126: ## Swapping lanes
 127: 
 128: Every role is a plug — the contracts attach to roles, not models. Today's
 129: lineup (session / cross-vendor reviewer / cheap implementer) is one
 130: configuration:
 131: 
 132: - **Implementer, Claude tier** — edit one line: `model:` in
 133:   `agents/implementer.md` frontmatter (any Claude tier is a drop-in). The contract (zero judgment calls, INPUT GAP rule, structured
 134:   report) stays identical whoever fills it.
 135: - **Implementer, cross-vendor** (the fable-advisor v3 Grok pattern) —
 136:   agent frontmatter only accepts Claude tiers, so a vendor swap uses the
 137:   supervisor pattern `agents/flash-implementer.md` implements (documented in `agents/implementer.md`'s Lane note): a cheap Claude
 138:   model supervises, delegates the body of work to the vendor CLI, and
 139:   re-runs verification itself.
 140: - **Cross-vendor reviewer** — a ONE-LINE swap: the canonical model id and
 141:   reasoning effort live solely in
 142:   `skills/multi-model-verify/references/model-prompting-notes.md`. The
 143:   behavioral runner and the drift watch parse those declarations at
 144:   runtime (failing loud if they vanish), and every instruction surface
 145:   (SKILL.md transport commands, doctor, drift-triage) reads them from
 146:   there; a consistency test forbids a hardcoded `-m` literal anywhere
 147:   else.
 148: - **Session** — whatever model runs the session; the debate rules and
 149:   final adjudication follow the seat automatically.
 150: 
 151: ## Requirements
 152: 
 153: - Claude Code on a current frontier model; superpowers plugin enabled
 154: - OpenAI codex CLI 0.144+ authenticated via ChatGPT sign-in, on a plan with
 155:   access to the canonical reviewer model (id, effort, and tier notes:
 156:   `skills/multi-model-verify/references/model-prompting-notes.md`)
 157: - `pwsh` (PowerShell 7) for the hook; Windows PowerShell 5.1 for the drift
 158:   watch scheduled task; Python 3.10+ for the evals
 159: 
 160: ## Install
 161: 
 162: Stable (any machine with git auth for this private repo):
 163: 
 164: ```
 165: claude plugin marketplace add Bmwascher/parallax
 166: claude plugin install parallax@parallax
 167: ```
 168: 
 169: Dev loop — Claude Code copies installs into a **versioned cache**, so
 170: checkout edits are NOT live until re-synced:
 171: 
 172: ```
 173: claude plugin marketplace add <path-to-this-checkout>
 174: claude plugin install parallax@parallax
 175: # after edits: bump .claude-plugin/plugin.json version, then
 176: claude plugin update parallax@parallax   # qualified name required
 177: # restart the Claude Code session to re-register hooks/skills
 178: ```
 179: 
 180: Forgetting a step here is the failure mode that looks like a plugin bug: a
 181: stale cache runs yesterday's skill, a missed restart leaves the hook
 182: unregistered. `/parallax:doctor` reports both, plus the fingerprint, the
 183: codex transport, quota headroom (best-effort, experimental), and any
 184: unresolved drift — in one table.
 185: 
 186: ## Verify
 187: 
 188: | Tier | Gate | Command | Runs |
 189: |---|---|---|---|
 190: | 1 — structure | Spec lint + security scan | `python evals/tools/skill_lint.py skills/multi-model-verify --strict` · `python evals/tools/skill_scanner.py skills` | CI + local |
 191: | 2 — routing | Trigger/routing evals | `python evals/tools/run_trigger_evals.py` | CI + local |
 192: | 2.5 — contract | Structural pytest suite (hook e2e under pwsh, pinned superpowers template fixture, transport/fallback/status-field pins) | `python -m pytest evals -q` | CI + local |
 193: | 3 — behavior | Real headless `claude -p` executor runs each case in a throwaway workspace (synthetic `References/DemoWidget` fixture; codex stripped from PATH for degraded cases), graded expectation-by-expectation by the cross-vendor reviewer — the executor's vendor never grades itself | `python evals/tools/run_behavioral_evals.py` | local only |
 194: 
 195: Tier 3 tests the **installed** plugin, not the checkout — run the dev-loop
 196: re-sync first. Lint/scan/trigger/pytest run in CI on every push.
 197: 
 198: Both modes are executed, not just described: the diff-mode case builds a real
 199: two-commit git history in the workspace (frozen plan → implementation with a
 200: planted throttle deviation from the reference) and hands the run the actual
 201: base/head SHAs, so a pass means the debate found the drift in a real diff.
 202: 
 203: The drift script has its own offline state machine —
 204: `evals/tools/drift_statemachine_tests.ps1` drives the **real**
 205: `tools/check-drift.ps1` through thirteen scenarios against stub CLIs and a
 206: throwaway clone: probe-failure carry-forward, the verdict trust matrix
 207: (`BLOCKED`, no verdict, trusted `NO-ACTION`), both halves of the toast
 208: matrix (CRITICAL dismissal toasts VERIFY, WARN-only dismissal toasts
 209: nothing), the pytest gate (a stub fix that *breaks the suite* must never
 210: commit), commit failure, off-grammar cross-review, an effective-route
 211: mismatch and a failed auth preflight (both read UNAVAILABLE, never a
 212: verdict), pending lifecycle, and the hung-agent kill. Slow and opt-in —
 213: run it whenever `check-drift.ps1` changes:
 214: 
 215: ```powershell
 216: .\evals\tools\drift_statemachine_tests.ps1
 217: # or through pytest:
 218: $env:PARALLAX_STATEMACHINE = "1"; python -m pytest evals -q
 219: ```
 220: 
 221: ## Drift protection
 222: 
 223: parallax's contract points at three moving targets it does not control.
 224: `tools/check-drift.ps1` watches all three; a clean run raises no toast
 225: (the report is still archived under `tools/drift-reports/` — gitignored,
 226: machine-local).
 227: 
 228: | Upstream | Risk | Check |
 229: |---|---|---|
 230: | superpowers | Template rewrite rots the hook fingerprint (`Senior Code Reviewer` / `Git Range to Review`) or the `Base:`/`Head:` extraction | Every run: hash the installed template against the pinned fixture; CRITICAL if the fingerprints are gone |
 231: | Claude Code | Surface changes — the Task→Agent tool rename (v2.1.63) silently killed the hook matcher once already | On version change: fetch the changelog slice between versions and grep for hook/plugin/matcher/skill/tool-rename keywords |
 232: | codex CLI | `exec` transport flags the skill's commands depend on | Every run: probe `--sandbox`, `--output-last-message`, model/config flags, and the `exec resume` subcommand |
 233: 
 234: ```
 235: powershell tools/check-drift.ps1            # one-shot
 236: powershell tools/check-drift.ps1 -Register  # weekly scheduled task (Tue 13:17)
 237: powershell tools/check-drift.ps1 -TestNotify  # toast wiring check
 238: ```
 239: 
 240: An unfetchable or not-yet-published changelog never advances the version
 241: snapshot — the watch retries next run rather than skipping past a version it
 242: could not inspect.
 243: 
 244: Findings don't wait for a human: on a findings-week the script feeds the
 245: report and the triage guide into a **headless Claude Code run**. Because
 246: the report embeds raw upstream text (changelog lines), that agent is
 247: treated as untrusted: it works in a **disposable git worktree** the script
 248: creates, and its isolation is two-layer — `--tools` makes shell, network,
 249: and subagent tools **unavailable** (so ambient settings can't resurrect
 250: them; even `python -c` would be arbitrary execution), while `--allowedTools`
 251: scopes write **approvals** to the worktree — and it is killed after 30
 252: minutes. The script then inspects the diff itself, re-runs the pytest
 253: gate, and only commits (on a `drift/<runid>` branch, never merged) when
 254: the gate is green and the commit verifiably landed. The toast reflects the verified outcome,
 255: so the only interruptions are actionable ones:
 256: 
 257: A `FIXES-APPLIED` diff also gets a **script-side cross-vendor review**
 258: (read-only, bounded) before the toast — preceded by the auth preflight,
 259: run with the env denylist, and accepted only when the codex header echoes
 260: the canonical route and the strict `REVIEW:` line closes the reply; the
 261: reviewer stays in the loop even unattended, and a failed review reads
 262: "cross-review UNAVAILABLE", never implied-reviewed. The final reviewer is the session, deferred to pickup:
 263: the merge happens in a session that adjudicates the cross-review verdict
 264: and the diff first (debate-protocol.md, Final adjudication), with the
 265: user's approval — nothing merges on the external reviewer's word alone.
 266: 
 267: | Outcome | Toast |
 268: |---|---|
 269: | `FIXES-APPLIED` + non-empty diff + gates green | "fix ready on `drift/<runid>` (gates green; cross-review: …) — review and merge" |
 270: | `NO-ACTION`, WARN-only noise, no diff | none (verdict archived in the report) |
 271: | `NO-ACTION` but a CRITICAL finding | "verify dismissal by hand" — a CRITICAL is never silently dismissed |
 272: | Gate failed, verdict/diff mismatch, timeout, `BLOCKED` | falls back to the manual toast: run `/parallax:drift-triage` yourself |
 273: 
 274: `-NoAutoTriage` disables the headless run (detection + manual toast only).
 275: `/parallax:drift-triage` remains available interactively — same guide the
 276: headless run follows.
 277: 
 278: Unresolved weeks can't fall out of the lifecycle: any run that ends in the
 279: manual toast or an open fix branch writes `tools/drift-pending.json`, and
 280: every later run re-toasts it until the branch is merged/discarded
 281: (auto-clears) or `/parallax:drift-triage` records a disposition —
 282: findings never depend on one noticed toast.
 283: 
 284: ## Attestation lane
 285: 
 286: A mode-`diff` terminal verdict is also recorded mechanically (0.6.0):
 287: after the session's final adjudication — never from the reviewer's reply
 288: alone — `tools/write-attestation.ps1` writes a SHA-bound JSON record
 289: (repo, base/head SHAs, verdict, rounds, participants, route note) under
 290: the reviewed repo's `.git/parallax/attestations/`. The git dir, not the
 291: working tree: recording the verdict cannot move HEAD out from under its
 292: own SHA, and the record never ships in a commit.
 293: 
 294: `tools/verify-attestation.ps1` is the consumer: a `main` push is attested
 295: when the pushed sha carries a gate-satisfying record (fast-forward), or
 296: when a merge commit's parent2 carries one **against parent1 as its base**
 297: — extra commits, a rebase, or a squash after the review all break the
 298: match and correctly force re-review. Gate-satisfying means verdict PASS
 299: **and** `verification_status: FULL` **and** the confirmed route note — a
 300: DEGRADED or unconfirmed-route PASS is rejected mechanically, not just in
 301: skill prose. The pre-push hooks (this repo's `.githooks/pre-push`;
 302: adapters in consuming repos) call it and **warn, never block** in v1 —
 303: the warning stream is how the lane earns a blocking future.
 304: 
 305: Enforcement is deliberately **local pre-push lanes only** (re-adjudicated
 306: 2026-07-19 after the reviewer flagged the gap): the records live under
 307: `.git` and never ship, so a GitHub-CI check would need an attestation
 308: carrier (git note, PR metadata, or an uploaded artifact) — deferred until
 309: a PR-based merge flow exists; today both repos merge locally, so pre-push
 310: IS the integration boundary.
 311: 
 312: ## Application checkpoint
 313: 
 314: The debate has a hard contract; without one, the **application phase**
 315: reverts to act-immediately bias — a verdict lands and the session starts
 316: editing with no record between "review concluded" and "diffs happening"
 317: (0.7.0, from a live failure observed in a distilled-skill setting). The
 318: checkpoint (`skills/multi-model-verify/references/application-checkpoint.md`)
 319: makes the missing transitions explicit —
 320: `reviewed → dispositioned → authorized → applied → reverified` — and is
 321: what authorizes touching files, not the verdict.
 322: 
 323: Before the first fix edit the session writes a checkpoint artifact under
 324: the reviewed repo's `.git/parallax/application-checkpoints/`: the reviewed
 325: range and outcome, a disposition per finding, one row per exact file with
 326: its intended **postcondition** (outcomes, never pseudocode), the
 327: verification plan, and an authorization line that either stops for the
 328: user or **quotes the pre-authorizing instruction verbatim**. Scope growth
 329: invalidates the authorization until the checkpoint is amended. At
 330: attestation time `-CheckpointFile` binds the artifact's hash plus the
 331: emitter-computed changed-path set into the record, and the verifier
 332: rejects a record whose path set no longer matches its range. The
 333: behavioral suite grades the contract end-to-end: a mutation-enabled eval
 334: lane (Edit/Write, deliberately no shell) checks the checkpoint precedes
 335: the first edit, refuted findings get no file plan, and the applied edits
 336: match the stated postconditions.
 337: 
 338: ## Pattern lineage
 339: 
 340: Advisor/evals ideas from
 341: [awesome-llm-apps agent_skills](https://github.com/Shubhamsaboo/awesome-llm-apps);
 342: plugin + self-marketplace shape from
 343: [fable-advisor](https://github.com/DannyMac180/fable-advisor); vendored eval
 344: tooling attribution in `evals/tools/LICENSE-THIRD-PARTY.md`. MIT licensed.
===== skills\multi-model-verify\references\frozen-plan-format.md =====
   1: # Frozen plan format
   2: 
   3: The debate's output is a superpowers-compatible implementation plan. The
   4: implementer (the pinned lane in `agents/`, or the session model, via superpowers
   5: subagent-driven-development or executing-plans) follows it with **zero
   6: judgment calls** — anything the plan leaves open is a plan defect, found in
   7: mode `diff` as drift.
   8: 
   9: ## Base format
  10: 
  11: Follow the superpowers writing-plans template exactly (header with Goal /
  12: Architecture / Tech Stack / Global Constraints; bite-sized checkbox tasks
  13: with exact Files / Interfaces / complete code / exact commands / expected
  14: output; no placeholders). Every task's verification command must be able
  15: to FAIL: the debate checks each one for oracle adequacy — a proof that
  16: would pass while the feature is broken (a compile check standing in for a
  17: behavior check, a test that never exercises the changed path) is a plan
  18: defect, found in the debate, not in production. Save location: the
  19: superpowers default
  20: `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`, unless the project
  21: overrides it (example: KitnEssentials uses `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`).
  22: 
  23: Port-specific Global Constraints to copy in verbatim when the work is a
  24: port (KitnDev-family example — adapt the specifics per project):
  25: 
  26: - Project naming/style conventions the port must follow (KE example:
  27:   KE namespace, flat DB keys, `KE:ApplyFontToText` for SOFTOUTLINE-capable
  28:   text, string literals only — see the module-port skill).
  29: - Reference source and version the port tracks:
  30:   `References/<name>/` at the fidelity the debate agreed on (faithful core
  31:   logic; project-specific deviations listed one per line WITH the debate
  32:   round that approved each).
  33: - Platform/API constraints the debate verified, each with its citation
  34:   (KE example: WoW 12.0 secret-value rules).
  35: 
  36: ## Debate record appendix (REQUIRED)
  37: 
  38: Every frozen plan ends with:
  39: 
  40: ```markdown
  41: ---
  42: 
  43: ## Debate record
  44: 
  45: **Participants:** <session model> (session) / <reviewer model> (codex exec, session <id>)
  46: **Rounds used:** N of CAP
  47: **Outcome:** converged | converged with amendments | escalated
  48: **Verification status:** FULL | DEGRADED
  49: **Degradation:** none | codex-missing | model-rejected | auth-expired | quota-exhausted | continuity-lost | <class>
  50: **Authorized by:** n/a | user at round N | not-authorized
  51: **Raw rounds:** <paths to the verbatim round replies/transcripts> | not retained
  52: 
  53: ### Resolved points
  54: | # | Claim | Raised by | Outcome | Evidence |
  55: |---|-------|-----------|---------|----------|
  56: | 1 | ...   | reviewer  | accepted into Task 3 | References/<addon>/<file>:<line> |
  57: | 2 | ...   | session   | refuted  | .wow-api-reference/<file> |
  58: | 3 | ...   | reviewer  | struck (no citation) | — |
  59: 
  60: ### Escalated points (user-decided)
  61: | # | Question | Session position | Reviewer position | Owner's call |
  62: |---|----------|------------------|-------------------|--------------|
  63: 
  64: ### Degraded-mode note
  65: (required whenever Verification status is DEGRADED — name what was skipped,
  66: why, and the recorded user authorization. The Participants line must name
  67: the actual participants, never the placeholder template line.)
  68: ```
  69: 
  70: The three status fields are structured on purpose: mode `diff` parses
  71: `Verification status` to enforce the degraded-plan poisoning rule (see
  72: SKILL.md) — prose notes cannot be enforced. `Raw rounds` records where the
  73: verbatim reviewer replies live (scratchpad transcripts are temporary — if
  74: they were not copied somewhere durable, say `not retained`): the summary
  75: tables above are the adjudication, not the provenance, and a later dispute
  76: about what the reviewer actually said needs the raw text or an honest
  77: "gone".
  78: 
  79: The appendix is the audit trail: mode `diff` re-reads it to know which
  80: deviations were approved and which claims were struck. A frozen plan without
  81: the appendix was not verified — treat it as an unfrozen draft.
  82: 
  83: ## Freezing
  84: 
  85: A plan is frozen when: both sides verdict PASS — or the final round's FIXes
  86: are accepted on the record (converged with amendments, per
  87: debate-protocol.md) — with any escalations carrying the user's recorded
  88: call, the writing-plans self-review checklist has run, and the file is
  89: saved. After freezing, changes require reopening the debate
  90: (a new round appended to the record) — the implementer never edits the plan.

+Amended by design spec 2026-07-25 (advisory review B1-B8): these tests pin
+the agent file's contract text so drift in the dispatch recipe, route
+check, forbidden-bypass class, or report format fails offline with zero
+CLI calls. The two agent files are the only allowed homes for the
+implementer model literal.
+"""
+import re
+from pathlib import Path
+
+REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+FLASH = REPO / "agents" / "flash-implementer.md"
+CLASSIC = REPO / "agents" / "implementer.md"
+CANONICAL_ID = "gemini-3.6-flash-medium"
+SHARED_START = "<!-- shared-contract:start -->"
+SHARED_END = "<!-- shared-contract:end -->"
+
+
+def _read(p):
+    return p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+
+
+def _frontmatter(text):
+    m = re.match(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
+    assert m, "missing frontmatter"
+    return m.group(1)
+
+
+def test_flash_frontmatter_pins_model_and_tools():
+    fm = _frontmatter(_read(FLASH))
+    assert re.search(r"^model: haiku$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
+    m = re.search(r"^tools: (.+)$", fm, re.MULTILINE)
+    assert m, "tools allowlist missing"
+    tools = [t.strip() for t in m.group(1).split(",")]
+    assert sorted(tools) == ["Bash", "Glob", "Grep", "Read"]
+
+
+def test_flash_dispatch_contract():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    assert "--model " + CANONICAL_ID in body
+    assert "--add-dir" in body
+    assert "--log-file" in body
+    assert "AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md" in body
+    # stdin is probed-dead in print mode; the body must not suggest it
+    assert "stdin" not in body.lower() or "does not reach" in body.lower()
+
+
+def test_flash_route_check_strings():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    assert 'Print mode: starting' in body
+    assert 'model="' + CANONICAL_ID + '"' in body
+    assert "Propagating selected model override" in body
+    assert "requested and propagated" in body
+    assert "used and confirmed" not in body.replace(
+        'never "used and confirmed"', "")
+    # log/tree corroboration rule (advisory review B2 amendment)
+    assert "every path git status reports changed must appear in the agy log" in body.lower()
+
+
+def test_flash_preflight_pins():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    assert "agy models" in body
+    assert "trustedWorkspaces" in body
+    assert "allow rule" in body  # settings-rule absence assertion
+
+
+def test_flash_forbidden_bypass_class():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    assert "--dangerously-skip-permissions" in body
+    idx = body.find("--dangerously-skip-permissions")
+    window = body[max(0, idx - 200):idx + 200].lower()
+    assert "never" in window or "forbidden" in window
+    assert "persisted" in body and "settings" in body
+
+
+def test_flash_report_headings():
+    body = _read(FLASH)
+    for heading in ("**STATUS:**", "**ROUTE:**", "**FILES CHANGED:**",
+                    "**VERIFICATION:**", "**DEVIATIONS:**"):
+        assert heading in body, heading
+    # the four shared headings are pinned in BOTH files so a unilateral
+    # rename in implementer.md cannot pass the suite (ROUTE is
+    # lane-specific to the flash file)
+    classic = _read(CLASSIC)
+    for heading in ("**STATUS:**", "**FILES CHANGED:**",
+                    "**VERIFICATION:**", "**DEVIATIONS:**"):
+        assert heading in classic, heading
+
+
+def _shared_block(text, path):
+    assert SHARED_START in text and SHARED_END in text, (
+        "missing shared-contract markers in " + str(path))
+    return text.split(SHARED_START)[1].split(SHARED_END)[0]
+
+
+def test_shared_contract_parity():
+    # byte-identical shared block; ROUTE is lane-specific and lives
+    # outside the block (spec section 1)
+    assert _shared_block(_read(FLASH), FLASH) == _shared_block(
+        _read(CLASSIC), CLASSIC)
+
+
+def test_classic_lane_note_retired_stale_claim():
+    body = _read(CLASSIC)
+    assert "Nothing else in the plugin references" not in body
+    assert "flash-implementer" in body
+
+
+SWEEP_GLOBS = [
+    "skills/**/*.md", "commands/*.md", "tools/*.ps1", "hooks/*",
+    "evals/**/*.py", "evals/**/*.json", "evals/**/*.ps1",
+    "README.md", "CLAUDE.md", "agents/*.md",
+]
+# The two agent files are the contract homes; this test file necessarily
+# carries the literal as its enforcement pin.
+ALLOWED = {FLASH.resolve(), CLASSIC.resolve(),
+           Path(__file__).resolve()}
+
+
+def test_flash_literal_single_source():
+    offenders = []
+    for pattern in SWEEP_GLOBS:
+        for p in REPO.glob(pattern):
+            if p.resolve() in ALLOWED:
+                continue
+            if "gemini-3.6-flash" in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8",
+                                                 errors="replace"):
+                offenders.append(str(p))
+    assert offenders == []
+
+
+def test_sonnet_implementer_literals_removed():
+    readme = _read(REPO / "README.md")
+    assert "currently `model: sonnet`" not in readme
+    # absence pins alone are vacuous against line-wrapped source; the
+    # presence pins below are the real oracles for Task 3's rewrites
+    assert "`haiku`/`opus` are drop-ins" not in readme
+    assert "any Claude tier is a drop-in" in readme
+    fpf = _read(REPO / "skills" / "multi-model-verify" / "references"
+                / "frozen-plan-format.md")
+    assert "Sonnet 5" not in fpf
+    assert "the pinned lane in `agents/`" in fpf

+---
+name: flash-implementer
+description: Zero-judgment Flash implementer for frozen-plan tasks. Use when executing build tasks from a debate-frozen implementation plan - give it ONE task's verbatim text plus the plan's Global Constraints and a log-file path. It delegates ALL code-writing to Gemini 3.6 Flash via the Antigravity CLI headlessly, verifies route and authorship evidence, runs the task's verification itself, and reports. It never types repo code and never makes design decisions.
+model: haiku
+tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
+---
+
+# Flash implementer (agy wrapper)
+
+You supervise ONE task from a frozen implementation plan. Gemini 3.6 Flash
+does ALL the typing through the Antigravity CLI (`agy`); you do preflight,
+dispatch, evidence checks, verification, and honest reporting. You never
+edit or create repo files yourself — your tool grant has no Edit or Write,
+and using Bash to write repo content is equally forbidden: a changed file
+the agy log cannot account for fails the task.
+
+<!-- shared-contract:start -->
+## The contract
+
+- Build exactly what the task says: the files it lists, the code it shows,
+  the commands it specifies. Nothing else.
+- No improvements, no drive-by refactors, no added error handling, no scope
+  adjustments. A deviation is a defect even when it looks better — the diff
+  gets checked against the plan afterward, and unexplained drift fails it.
+- **INPUT GAP rule:** if the task references a file, interface, value, or
+  convention that is not in your brief and not discoverable at the exact
+  path the task names, STOP and report the gap. Never invent or guess the
+  missing piece.
+- Run the task's verification commands yourself and read the output. Never
+  claim completion without re-running verification — "should work" means
+  the task is not done.
+<!-- shared-contract:end -->
+
+## Inputs (from the dispatching controller)
+
+- The task's verbatim text and the plan's Global Constraints.
+- The workspace directory (this cycle: the main checkout only).
+- A log-file path OUTSIDE the workspace (the controller owns it; you never
+  place logs in the repo tree).
+
+## Preflight (all three must pass BEFORE dispatch)
+
+1. `agy models` (binary at `$LOCALAPPDATA/agy/bin/agy.exe`) — output must
+   contain `gemini-3.6-flash-medium`. Anything else (missing binary,
+   sign-out, missing model) is blocked.
+2. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — `trustedWorkspaces` must
+   contain the workspace directory. If not: blocked, and the report quotes
+   the fix ("run one interactive `agy` session in the workspace and approve
+   trust").
+3. The same settings file must carry NO per-tool allow rule targeting the
+   workspace (for example `write_file(...)`). A persisted settings rule is
+   the durable, call-site-invisible bypass class — its absence is the
+   load-bearing permission control. If present: blocked, quoting the rule.
+
+## Dispatch
+
+1. Write the brief to `<workspace>/AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md` with a Bash heredoc:
+   the task's verbatim text, the Global Constraints, and the exact files
+   list. (stdin does not reach the model in print mode — probed 2026-07-25;
+   the workspace brief file is the delivery mechanism.)
+2. Run (single line):
+   `agy -p "Read the file AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md in the workspace and execute it exactly." --model gemini-3.6-flash-medium --add-dir <workspace> --log-file <log-path>`
+3. Delete `AGY-TASK-BRIEF.md` immediately after agy exits, BEFORE any
+   evidence check, so it never appears in `git status`.
+
+## Route and authorship checks (every run, on the log file)
+
+- `Print mode: starting` line present containing
+  `model="gemini-3.6-flash-medium"`.
+- `Propagating selected model override` line present (presence only — its
+  display label is not matched).
+- Log/tree corroboration: every path git status reports changed must appear in the agy log as a file Flash touched. A changed file the log never mentions means someone other than Flash typed it — blocked, no matter what the tests say.
+- This evidence is client-side: report the route as **requested and
+  propagated**, never "used and confirmed". Server-side substitution is
+  not detectable from this evidence class.
+
+## Failure handling — loud, never silent
+
+Blocked (quote the exact output) on: any preflight failure, the print-mode
+soft-deny line ("auto-denied"), nonzero exit, a missing or mismatched
+route line, a corroboration mismatch, or writes diverted to agy's internal
+scratch (expected files absent from the tree). Never retry with
+`--dangerously-skip-permissions` — that flag is forbidden in this lane, as
+is ANY approval-bypass flag or persisted per-tool allow rule added to agy
+settings. Never complete the work yourself: rerouting a blocked task to a
+Claude tier is the user's decision, recorded in the plan's Escalated
+points — not yours.
+
+## Report format (your final message)
+
+- **STATUS:** done | blocked | INPUT GAP: <exactly what is missing>
+- **ROUTE:** the resolved model ID as requested and propagated, plus the
+  retained log file's path
+- **FILES CHANGED:** actual paths from `git status` — on blocked, STILL
+  list every path Flash already touched so the session can revert a
+  partial write
+- **VERIFICATION:** each command you ran yourself, with its real output
+  (condensed)
+- **DEVIATIONS:** must be "none" — anything else means you stopped and are
+  explaining why the task could not be built as written
+
+## Lane note
+
+This agent pins the Flash implementation lane. Canonical model literal:
+`gemini-3.6-flash-medium` (Gemini 3.6 Flash, medium reasoning effort,
+Antigravity CLI resolved ID). The literal lives ONLY here;
+`implementer.md` pins its own lane's model in its frontmatter and Lane
+note — every other surface points at the agent files. Trust is per-directory and interactive-only, so this lane runs in
+the main checkout this cycle — a worktree trust story is future work.

===== frozen-plan-format at candidate head 624d52f =====
# Frozen plan format

The debate's output is a superpowers-compatible implementation plan. The
implementer (Sonnet 5 or the session model, via superpowers
subagent-driven-development or executing-plans) follows it with **zero
judgment calls** — anything the plan leaves open is a plan defect, found in
mode `diff` as drift.

## Base format

Follow the superpowers writing-plans template exactly (header with Goal /
Architecture / Tech Stack / Global Constraints; bite-sized checkbox tasks
with exact Files / Interfaces / complete code / exact commands / expected
output; no placeholders). Every task's verification command must be able
to FAIL: the debate checks each one for oracle adequacy — a proof that
would pass while the feature is broken (a compile check standing in for a
behavior check, a test that never exercises the changed path) is a plan
defect, found in the debate, not in production. Save location: the
superpowers default
`docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`, unless the project
overrides it (example: KitnEssentials uses `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`).

Port-specific Global Constraints to copy in verbatim when the work is a
port (KitnDev-family example — adapt the specifics per project):

- Project naming/style conventions the port must follow (KE example:
  KE namespace, flat DB keys, `KE:ApplyFontToText` for SOFTOUTLINE-capable
  text, string literals only — see the module-port skill).
- Reference source and version the port tracks:
  `References/<name>/` at the fidelity the debate agreed on (faithful core
  logic; project-specific deviations listed one per line WITH the debate
  round that approved each).
- Platform/API constraints the debate verified, each with its citation
  (KE example: WoW 12.0 secret-value rules).

## Debate record appendix (REQUIRED)

Every frozen plan ends with:

```markdown
---

## Debate record

**Participants:** <session model> (session) / <reviewer model> (codex exec, session <id>)
**Rounds used:** N of CAP
**Outcome:** converged | converged with amendments | escalated
**Verification status:** FULL | DEGRADED
**Degradation:** none | codex-missing | model-rejected | auth-expired | quota-exhausted | continuity-lost | <class>
**Authorized by:** n/a | user at round N | not-authorized
**Raw rounds:** <paths to the verbatim round replies/transcripts> | not retained

### Resolved points
| # | Claim | Raised by | Outcome | Evidence |
|---|-------|-----------|---------|----------|
| 1 | ...   | reviewer  | accepted into Task 3 | References/<addon>/<file>:<line> |
| 2 | ...   | session   | refuted  | .wow-api-reference/<file> |
| 3 | ...   | reviewer  | struck (no citation) | — |

### Escalated points (user-decided)
| # | Question | Session position | Reviewer position | Owner's call |
|---|----------|------------------|-------------------|--------------|

### Degraded-mode note
(required whenever Verification status is DEGRADED — name what was skipped,
why, and the recorded user authorization. The Participants line must name
the actual participants, never the placeholder template line.)
```

The three status fields are structured on purpose: mode `diff` parses
`Verification status` to enforce the degraded-plan poisoning rule (see
SKILL.md) — prose notes cannot be enforced. `Raw rounds` records where the
verbatim reviewer replies live (scratchpad transcripts are temporary — if
they were not copied somewhere durable, say `not retained`): the summary
tables above are the adjudication, not the provenance, and a later dispute
about what the reviewer actually said needs the raw text or an honest
"gone".

The appendix is the audit trail: mode `diff` re-reads it to know which
deviations were approved and which claims were struck. A frozen plan without
the appendix was not verified — treat it as an unfrozen draft.

## Freezing

A plan is frozen when: both sides verdict PASS — or the final round's FIXes
are accepted on the record (converged with amendments, per
debate-protocol.md) — with any escalations carrying the user's recorded
call, the writing-plans self-review checklist has run, and the file is
saved. After freezing, changes require reopening the debate
(a new round appended to the record) — the implementer never edits the plan.
===== debate protocol at candidate head 624d52f relevant tail =====

  ## Convergence and the round cap
  
> - Converged: both sides verdict PASS in the same round.
  - **Converged with amendments**: the final round's only outstanding verdicts
    are FIXes whose fixes the other side accepts on the record. Record each
    accepted fix in the debate record and treat the plan as converged — an
    accepted FIX is agreement, not a dispute for the user to settle. (This
    matters whenever a FIX lands in the last round before the cap.)
  - Round cap: **4 exchanges** by default (caller may raise or lower it).
    Hitting the cap is not failure — it is the signal to stop spending tokens
    on an argument evidence hasn't settled.
    the user's step).
  
> ## Final adjudication (the session's last step)
  
> The chain never ends on the external reviewer's verdict. After the
  reviewer's final round — including a re-review of fixes — the SESSION
  performs one closing step before anything merges or freezes:
  
  1. Verify each finding from that final round against the live repo (read
     the cited lines; run the gates if implicated) — a final-round finding is
     itself an unverified claim until checked.
  2. Accept with evidence, refute with evidence, or ESCALATE to the user.
     Nothing is adopted or dismissed on authority.
> 3. Emit the terminal verdict. A reviewer PASS/FIX is input to this step,
     never the decision itself.
  4. If that verdict requires changes this session will apply itself, the
     chain is NOT over: the application checkpoint
     (references/application-checkpoint.md) precedes the FIRST file edit —
     the checkpoint, not the verdict, is what authorizes touching files.
     After applying, execute the checkpoint's verification plan and
     re-review the fixed range; only the post-re-review terminal PASS
     closes the chain.

---
name: writing-plans
description: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code
---

# Writing Plans

## Overview

Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming the engineer has zero context for our codebase and questionable taste. Document everything they need to know: which files to touch for each task, code, testing, docs they might need to check, how to test it. Give them the whole plan as bite-sized tasks. DRY. YAGNI. TDD. Frequent commits.

Assume they are a skilled developer, but know almost nothing about our toolset or problem domain. Assume they don't know good test design very well.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the writing-plans skill to create the implementation plan."

**Context:** If working in an isolated worktree, it should have been created via the `superpowers:using-git-worktrees` skill at execution time.

**Save plans to:** `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`
- (User preferences for plan location override this default)

## Scope Check

If the spec covers multiple independent subsystems, it should have been broken into sub-project specs during brainstorming. If it wasn't, suggest breaking this into separate plans — one per subsystem. Each plan should produce working, testable software on its own.

## File Structure

Before defining tasks, map out which files will be created or modified and what each one is responsible for. This is where decomposition decisions get locked in.

- Design units with clear boundaries and well-defined interfaces. Each file should have one clear responsibility.
- You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. Prefer smaller, focused files over large ones that do too much.
- Files that change together should live together. Split by responsibility, not by technical layer.
- In existing codebases, follow established patterns. If the codebase uses large files, don't unilaterally restructure - but if a file you're modifying has grown unwieldy, including a split in the plan is reasonable.

This structure informs the task decomposition. Each task should produce self-contained changes that make sense independently.

## Task Right-Sizing

A task is the smallest unit that carries its own test cycle and is worth a
fresh reviewer's gate. When drawing task boundaries: fold setup,
configuration, scaffolding, and documentation steps into the task whose
deliverable needs them; split only where a reviewer could meaningfully
reject one task while approving its neighbor. Each task ends with an
independently testable deliverable.

## Bite-Sized Task Granularity

**Each step is one action (2-5 minutes):**
- "Write the failing test" - step
- "Run it to make sure it fails" - step
- "Implement the minimal code to make the test pass" - step
- "Run the tests and make sure they pass" - step
- "Commit" - step

## Plan Document Header

**Every plan MUST start with this header:**

```markdown
# [Feature Name] Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** [One sentence describing what this builds]

**Architecture:** [2-3 sentences about approach]

**Tech Stack:** [Key technologies/libraries]

## Global Constraints

[The spec's project-wide requirements — version floors, dependency limits,
naming and copy rules, platform requirements — one line each, with exact
values copied verbatim from the spec. Every task's requirements implicitly
include this section.]

---
```

## Task Structure

````markdown
### Task N: [Component Name]

**Files:**
- Create: `exact/path/to/file.py`
- Modify: `exact/path/to/existing.py:123-145`
- Test: `tests/exact/path/to/test.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: [what this task uses from earlier tasks — exact signatures]
- Produces: [what later tasks rely on — exact function names, parameter
  and return types. A task's implementer sees only their own task; this
  block is how they learn the names and types neighboring tasks use.]

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
def test_specific_behavior():
    result = function(input)
    assert result == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: FAIL with "function not defined"

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

```python
def function(input):
    return expected
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: PASS

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add tests/path/test.py src/path/file.py
git commit -m "feat: add specific feature"
```
````

## No Placeholders

Every step must contain the actual content an engineer needs. These are **plan failures** — never write them:
- "TBD", "TODO", "implement later", "fill in details"
- "Add appropriate error handling" / "add validation" / "handle edge cases"
- "Write tests for the above" (without actual test code)
- "Similar to Task N" (repeat the code — the engineer may be reading tasks out of order)
- Steps that describe what to do without showing how (code blocks required for code steps)
- References to types, functions, or methods not defined in any task

## Self-Review

After writing the complete plan, look at the spec with fresh eyes and check the plan against it. This is a checklist you run yourself — not a subagent dispatch.

**1. Spec coverage:** Skim each section/requirement in the spec. Can you point to a task that implements it? List any gaps.

**2. Placeholder scan:** Search your plan for red flags — any of the patterns from the "No Placeholders" section above. Fix them.

**3. Type consistency:** Do the types, method signatures, and property names you used in later tasks match what you defined in earlier tasks? A function called `clearLayers()` in Task 3 but `clearFullLayers()` in Task 7 is a bug.

If you find issues, fix them inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on. If you find a spec requirement with no task, add the task.

## Execution Handoff

After saving the plan, offer execution choice:

**"Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/<filename>.md`. Two execution options:**

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** - I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Inline Execution** - Execute tasks in this session using executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

**Which approach?"**

**If Subagent-Driven chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
- Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review

**If Inline Execution chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:executing-plans
- Batch execution with checkpoints for review
